# Gantry Augmentation â€” Problem Log

This document records the structural problems encountered when applying Jan's parallel
ANN augmentation framework to the gantry system, what was tried, and where the problem
currently stands.

---

## 1. Jan's Parallel Routing (the reference method)

Jan's framework for dynamic parallel augmentation is defined in
`scripts/ecc_2025/msd_ndof_interconnect_dynamic.py`, lines 91â€“98 (read directly):

```python
    interconnect.connect_block_signals(ANN_state_block, ["x", "u"], ["xp"])

    interconnect.connect_signals("x", physical_state_model_block, "concat", selection_matrix(np.array([0,1,2,3]), nxd))
    interconnect.connect_block_signals(physical_state_model_block, ["u"], [])
    interconnect.connect_signals(physical_state_model_block, "xp", "additive", expansion_matrix(np.array([0,1,2,3]), nxd))

    interconnect.connect_signals("x", physical_output_model_block, "concat", selection_matrix(np.array([0,1,2,3]), nxd))
    interconnect.connect_block_signals(physical_output_model_block, ["u"], ["y"])
```

**What this does:**
- ANN sees the full state `x` and input `u`, outputs additive corrections to ALL `nxd` state rows (`["xp"]`)
- Physical state block (MSD) also updates rows `[0,1,2,3]` additively
- Output `y` comes from the physical output block only (fixed linear map)

**Why it works for MSD:**  
The MSD has spring stiffness K > 0. Any additive position correction from the ANN is
bounded by the spring: if the ANN pushes position up, the spring pushes back. Gradient
magnitude saturates with rollout length T (oscillatory cancellation). The ANN can learn
non-zero corrections at all rollout lengths without causing drift.

Diagnostic: `scripts/gantry/augmentation-error/diag_spring_stiffness.py`  
Result: K=0 â†’ position grows O(TÂ·Îµ), gradient grows without bound with T.
K>0 â†’ position oscillates and stays bounded, gradient saturates.

---

## 2. K = 0 Problem (gantry axes)

The gantry X, Y, and bridge axes are electric servo drives with **no spring stiffness
and no passive damping** (K = 0, C â‰ˆ 0 for the physical position equations). These axes
are pure double integrators.

**Consequence of applying Jan's routing to the gantry:**

- ANN outputs additive correction Îµ to a velocity state row each step
- Velocity correction integrates into position: Î”q = Îµ Â· N_steps Â· Ts
- Position correction directly: Î”q grows O(N Â· Îµ)
- Under K = 0 there is no restoring force â€” corrections accumulate without bound

At training rollout nf = 400 (100 ms): drift is O(400 Â· Ts Â· Îµ) â€” small for small Îµ.  
At validation on the full trajectory (sim-RMS, N â‰ˆ 8000 steps): drift is 20Ã— larger.  
This is the root cause of the original training failure (SLURM job 68458):
**best checkpoint = epoch 0 in all 20 training epochs.**

---

## 3. Training / Validation Horizon Mismatch (original failure mode)

**Setup (job 68458):** training nf = 400 (windowed), validation = sim-RMS (full trajectory).

- Training sees 400-step windows â†’ ANN learns corrections that look good at 400 steps
- Validation applies the model to the full trajectory (8 000+ steps)
- K = 0 drift at 8 000 steps is 20Ã— larger than at 400 steps
- Every epoch makes validation worse than epoch 0 â†’ best model = untrained model

**Fix applied:** switch validation to `'400-step-average-RMS'` (windowed, matching
training nf). This removes the horizon mismatch. Windowed validation and training now
see the same rollout length.

---

## 4. Model A â€” C_aug Dead Zone (first routing attempt)

**Architecture:** ANN â†’ augmented state rows [6, 7] only; a learned `Parameterized_Linear_Output_Block` (C_aug) maps x_aug â†’ y contribution.

**Problem:** C_aug is initialized near zero (Frobenius norm â‰ˆ 1e-2). The gradient of the
loss with respect to ANN parameters flows through C_aug. Near-zero C_aug â‰ˆ 0 means
near-zero gradient â†’ ANN receives no training signal.

Diagnostic: `scripts/gantry/augmentation-error/diag_gradient_routing.py`  
Result: Model A ANN gradient norm = 1.04e-2. Model B (velocity routing) = 2.85e-1.
Ratio B/A = 27Ã—. C_aug confirmed as the gradient bottleneck.

---

## 5. Model B â€” Velocity Routing (second attempt, superseded)

**Architecture:** ANN â†’ velocity rows [3, 4, 5] + augmented rows [6, 7] via
`expansion_matrix(VEL_AUG_IX, nxd)`. C_aug removed; only fixed `Linear_Output_Block`.

**Result:** Gradient dead zone resolved (27Ã— stronger gradient). However, training still
failed: best checkpoint remained epoch 0. Windowed validation was not yet in place; the
K = 0 horizon mismatch still dominated.

**Note:** routing to velocity-only rows reduces but does not eliminate K = 0 drift.
Velocity corrections still integrate into position errors over N steps (O(N Â· Ts Â· Îµ)).
This approach was superseded before a proper windowed-validation run was attempted.

Decision log: D-066 (superseded by D-067).

---

## 6. Jan's `bestfit` Global Tracking Bug

**Discovery:** Jan's framework (`model_augmentation/fit_systems/interconnect.py`, line 426)
initialises `self.bestfit = float('inf')` in `init_model()` only â€” not at the start of
each `fit()` call. Each `fit()` compares new validation scores against the global minimum.

**Consequence for curriculum training:**

- Stage 1 sets bestfit = 0.05 (25-step-average-RMS, low because windowed)
- Stage 2 validation (50-step-average-RMS) scores â‰ˆ 0.07 â€” larger absolute value
- `if self.bestfit >= Loss_val` is never true in stage 2 â†’ checkpoint never saved
- At end of stage 2 `fit()`, best checkpoint is loaded â†’ **model reverts to stage 1 weights**
- Stages 2â€“6 effectively run training but discard all learned weights at the end of each stage

**Fix applied:** `fit_sys.bestfit = float('inf')` immediately before each stage's
`train_model()` call in `train_model_with_diagnostics()`. Each stage now tracks its own
best checkpoint independently.

---

## 7. FP Model Near-Perfect Accuracy (weak gradient signal)

**Observation:** Initial validation before any training:
`400-step-average-RMS = 0.00015686001` (windowed, FP model + zero ANN).

This is the FP model's prediction error on 400-step windows. The FP model is physics-based
and already extremely accurate. The ANN starts at zero (via `zero_init_feed_forward_nn`)
and needs to find corrections that improve on this near-perfect baseline.

**Consequence:** The gradient signal available to the ANN at nf = 400 is very small.
The dominant unmodeled dynamics (absorber coupling) contribute only a tiny fraction of
the output error at 400 steps. The ANN may fail to learn because the improvement
opportunity is below the effective noise floor of gradient descent.

This is the key structural difference from Jan's MSD benchmark, where the FP model is
intentionally undermodeled (2-DOF approximating 3-DOF system), providing a large
residual for the ANN to learn.

---

## 8. Absorber Dynamics Timescale

The absorber natural period is approximately 100 ms = 400 samples at 4 kHz. This means:

- nf < 400: absorber barely moves within the rollout window â†’ no gradient signal for absorber dynamics
- nf = 400: one full absorber oscillation visible â†’ first rollout length where learning is physically possible
- nf >> 400: absorber dynamics accumulate; stronger signal but K = 0 drift re-emerges for non-zero-mean corrections

Short curriculum stages (nf = 25, 50, 100, 200) do not contain absorber dynamics and
provide no useful training signal for the augmented states. The minimum useful rollout
is nf = 400.

---

## 9. Training Always Windowed (not the full trajectory)

Jan's framework trains on batches of nf-length windows extracted from the training
trajectories. The full training trajectory is never used in a single forward pass.
This is the same for both MSD and gantry.

- `'X-step-average-RMS'` validation: also windowed (rolling X-step windows on val set,
  state re-initialized from encoder at each window start)
- `'sim-RMS'` validation: full trajectory via `apply_experiment` (single initial condition,
  no state resets, errors compound)

The windowed validation does NOT test whether corrections generalize to the full
trajectory. Only sim-RMS does. Corrections learned at nf = 400 generalize to the full
trajectory only if they are zero-mean over the absorber period (so K = 0 drift cancels).

---

## 10. Current Status and Open Questions

**What is implemented (as of 2026-07-01):**

- Full-state routing (Jan's method): `ic.connect_block_signals(ann_block, ["x", "u"], ["xp"])`
- C_aug removed; single physical output block
- Windowed validation matching training nf at each curriculum stage
- `fit_sys.bestfit = float('inf')` reset before each stage
- 6-stage curriculum in code: nf 25â†’50â†’100â†’200â†’400 (windowed) â†’ 400 (sim-RMS)
- Per-stage checkpointing with SLURM job ID in filename
- Resume via `RESUME_CHECKPOINT` environment variable

**What is decided but not yet implemented:**

- D-068: stiffness-selective routing `STIFF_IX = [1, 4, 6, 7]` via `expansion_matrix`
  (Jan's `state_augment_specific_states` fix â€” see section 11)

**Open questions:**

1. Does stiffness-selective routing (D-068) eliminate the K=0 blowup under sim-RMS?
   This is the next experiment â€” single-stage sim-RMS run.
2. Will the ANN learn at all given the near-perfect FP model baseline (0.00015686 windowed)?
3. Do the multisine training trajectories sufficiently excite the absorber natural frequency?
   If not, delta_a â‰ˆ 0 throughout and there is nothing for the ANN to learn regardless of routing.
4. Is the windowed first stage still necessary after D-068 eliminates K=0 drift at X/Y?

## 11. Jan's Response and Proposed Fix (2026-07-01)

Email sent 2026-06-30 described the K=0 blowup, the K matrix, and the C_aug dead zone.
Jan replied 2026-07-01:

**Key points from Jan's reply:**

1. **Confirms accumulation hypothesis:** "het lijkt me wel voor de XY grid van de
   wirebonder, aangezien dat een integrator is" â€” the XY grid is an integrator and
   accumulation is expected. A disturbance test can verify this directly.

2. **Validates windowed validation:** suggests `"100-step-RMS"` as an alternative
   validation measure to see what happens before error accumulates. This is exactly the
   approach we independently arrived at (section 3).

3. **Concrete fix:** "je zou kunnen proberen alleen de state te augmenteren die wel een
   spring heeft. Dit kan met de `state_augment_specific_states` flag en de indices array
   eronder." Route ANN corrections ONLY to states with spring stiffness (K > 0).

**Proposed routing for the gantry:**

From the K matrix (transformed coords X, Theta, Y):
- X (index 0, 3): K = 0 â€” do NOT route
- Theta (index 1, 4): K = kb1+kb2 > 0 â€” route here
- Y (index 2, 5): K = 0 â€” do NOT route
- delta_a (index 6): absorber spring â€” route here
- vdelta_a (index 7): absorber spring â€” route here

`STIFF_IX = np.array([1, 4, 6, 7])`

**What changes in `build_model()`:**

Replace:
```python
ic.connect_block_signals(ann_block, ["x", "u"], ["xp"])   # full-state routing
```
With:
```python
ic.connect_block_signals(ann_block, ["x", "u"], [])
ic.connect_signals(ann_block, "xp", "additive", expansion_matrix(STIFF_IX, nxd))
```
ANN output width changes from `nxd = NX_PHYS + NX_ANN` to `len(STIFF_IX) = 4`.

**Trade-off:** absorber-to-X/Y coupling is not directly captured (X/Y rows excluded).
The absorber dynamics themselves (delta_a evolution) are still learned. This is a
physically motivated simplification: the dominant error from the absorber appears in its
own state evolution, not in the gantry translation states.

**Implementation note:** Jan refers to a `state_augment_specific_states` flag in his
framework. Inspect `model_augmentation/fit_systems/` to find the exact API before
implementing â€” do not reconstruct from first principles.

## 11b. Black-Box Diagnosis Session, 2026-08-11

Scope: `scripts/gantry/ann-blackbox/` only. No baseline, no interconnect, no projection.
Every number below is measured today; the per-run detail is in the Section 12 rows.

### The result

**The gantry black box does not fail for want of training. It fails because gradient descent on
the SUBNET objective never places poles at `z = 1`, and the gantry's X and Y have no stiffness
(`_K4` diagonal `[0, kb1+kb2, 0, KA]`), so those two modes are free rigid-body integrators.**

| arm | learned `max abs(lambda)` | modes > 0.999 | best sim-RMS | vs `mean_pred` | vs FP baseline |
|-|-|-|-|-|-|
| random init, `nf=400`, 140 upd | 0.67 - 0.82 | 0 | 0.1289 | 0.90x | 685x |
| random init, `nf=3700`, 110 upd | 0.63 - 0.67 | 0 | 0.1179 | 0.80x | 626x |
| BLA init, 5215 upd | 1.0018 | 2 | 0.03347 | 0.228x | 178x |
| **random init, 84,980 upd (SLURM 76175)** | **0.856 - 0.868** | **0** | **0.08632** | 0.587x | 458x |
| **BLA init, 85,610 upd (SLURM 76176)** | **1.000 - 1.019** | **2** | **0.00951** | **0.065x** | **50.5x** |
| true system | 1.0000 | 2 | | | |

**The decisive comparison is the last two rows: same 12 h, same ~85k updates, same seed, differing
only in initialisation. BLA is 9.1x better on best and 3.8x better on median, and BOTH arms are
converged (random's last-half log-log slope is `+0.049`, `R2 = 0.007`).** See the Section 12 paired
row. The `max abs(lambda)` for those two is unmeasured because only the logs were copied back.

Same data, same architecture, same objective, same seed; **only the initialisation differs.**
N4SID finds the integrators to within `1.755e-05` of `z = 1`; the network then keeps them through
5215 updates and scores 4x better. Per channel, X went from `1.03x` of its own signal std to
`0.28x` and Y from `4.67x` to `1.20x`. **Gradient descent cannot find the marginal modes; it can
hold them.**

**Mechanism: established by literature sweep, see "The mechanism" below. The obvious candidate is
refuted by our own data.** The tempting story is that the encoder re-initialises the state every
0.5 s window so the loss never requires memory longer than the window. That predicts a longer
window should help, and **`nf = 3700` (4.6 s, 9x longer) leaves the spectrum unchanged at
`0.63-0.67`**. So window length is not the mechanism, and Ribeiro et al.'s Theorem 1 explains why
it never could be (polynomial, not exponential, growth at the margin). What does survive is an
asymmetry in the METRIC rather than the optimiser: Jan's system is asymptotically stable
(`tau_max = 5.3 s` against a 200 s validation record), so in free run it forgets its initial
condition and state errors do not accumulate, whereas the gantry's integrators make any state error
permanent across the whole 12 s. That explains why free-run validation punishes us and not him.

### Eliminated, each by measurement

| candidate | how it was killed |
|-|-|
| undertrained | lr sweep 1e-3 / 3e-4 / 1e-4 all flatten at `0.128-0.133` by update 140-245 |
| optimiser divergence | the update-140 reversal recovers by update 245; the excursion was noise on three points |
| Y / operating-point coverage | 14 records spanning Y = -30..+30 made it **worse** (`rms_Y` 0.209 -> 0.247) |
| training horizon | `nf = 3700` (Beintema 3-tau at 800 Hz) leaves the spectrum unchanged, `max abs(lambda)` 0.63-0.67 |
| initial state / encoder | fitting ONLY `x0` with weights frozen changes nothing (0.8x and 1.0x) |
| oversampling | gantry arms run at 5.06 samples/period, LESS than Jan's own 8.67 |

### The mechanism (literature sweep, 2026-08-11)

Four-agent `deep-research` sweep, ~150 queries, TU/e browser access available. Full report in the
session transcript; the load-bearing citations are below. **The answer is two independent
representation failures, and neither is an optimisation-landscape effect in the sense we assumed.**

**A. The rolled-out, input-only objective is not an estimable object for this plant.** Three
literatures that do not cite one another reach the same conclusion, and all three fix it by feeding
outputs back:

| source | statement | our reading |
|-|-|-|
| Phan, Horta, Juang, Longman, *JOTA* 79(1):59-86, 1993, DOI `10.1007/BF00941887` (OKID; free at NASA NTRS `19920017294`) | "if the system is unstable or **marginally stable**, such a description is no longer possible", of a truncated input-output/Markov model | a finite-memory input-output description of our plant does not exist |
| Simchowitz, Boczar, Recht, COLT 2019, arXiv:1902.00768, **Theorem C.1** | with a unit-circle Jordan block of size `k`, minimal, and **zero process and sensor noise**, `norm(Ghat_LS - G*) >= C N^(k-1)` | at our `k = 2` the error lower bound **grows like `N`**: more data is provably worse. Their motivating example is literally `A = [[1, Delta], [0, 1]]` from `F = m xddot` |
| Yaghmaie, Esmzad, Modares, CDC 2025, DOI `10.1109/CDC57313.2025.11312621` | Cayley-Hamilton ARX in `n` past **outputs**; noise "does not grow with the rollout length `T`" | the input-only form's noise grows with `T_m` and `rho(A)` (their Remark 1); the output-feeding form does not |

This is the first mechanism that predicts **both** of our negative results at once: the 14-record
regression (`rms_Y` 0.209 -> 0.247) and the `nf = 3700` non-improvement. Our SUBNET **encoder** is
output-feeding and structurally sound, which is consistent with the frozen-`x0` result changing
nothing; the **decoder rolled out `nf` steps with no output feedback** is precisely the condemned
object. Simchowitz's fix is prefiltering on a subsampled output history (his Eq. 2.7), giving
consistency up to and including `rho(A) = 1` (his Corollary 2.2).

**B. `abs(lambda) = 1` is not reachable at finite parameter values in the standard parameterisations.**
`lambda = exp(-exp(nu_log))` maps onto the *open* interval, so the unit circle sits at
`nu_log = -inf`. Structurally the same pathology as the Cayley transform at `-1`, where Helfrich,
Willmott, Ye (ICML 2018, arXiv:1707.09520) measured pre-image entries of magnitude **447.212** and
concluded "**gradient descent algorithms will learn this `A` matrix very slowly, if at all**";
Lezcano-Casado and Martinez-Rubio (ICML 2019, arXiv:1901.08428) prove the Lie exponential is the
parameterisation without this defect. Measured confirmation that it bites in practice: Casoni,
Guidi, Melacci, Betti, Gori, ICANN 2025, DOI `10.1007/978-3-032-04558-4_43`, report that LRU and
G-LRU fail their long-horizon free-run task because "their exponential diagonal parameterization,
which places eigenvalues slightly inside the unit circle for stability, causes vanishing behavior
over long horizons", reaching "a steady zero state". Agarwal, Suo, Chen, Hazan (arXiv:2312.06837)
show an lr-and-parameterisation sweep failing on a marginally stable LDS, which is our lr sweep in
a different model class.

Layer A explains why more data and a longer horizon hurt. Layer B explains the hard ceiling at
0.999 that no learning rate moved.

**The corrected MSD morph arms are a controlled measurement of layer B specifically, and they fit it
better than they fit a collapse story.** Arm B (free-floating, absolute noise, see Corrections) went
to `max abs(lambda) = 0.96008` with **zero** modes above 0.999 against a true two, i.e. it moved
*toward* the circle and stopped short. That is exactly what an `exp`-type or unconstrained
parameterisation predicts (approach from inside, never arrive), and it is not what a landscape
barrier or a divergence-avoidance story predicts. It also matches Wang and Li's Remark C.1, that when
the approximation is unstable the weights can only approach the boundary from the stable side, and
Casoni et al.'s measured LRU vanishing at long horizon.

**Two candidates are now dead, both by our own numbers.**
- *AR-by-OLS downward bias* (Kendall 1954, Marriott and Pope 1954, Andrews, *Econometrica* 61(1),
  1993, DOI `10.2307/2951781`). Direction right, magnitude wrong: `-(1+3rho)/T` at `T = 400` gives
  `-0.010` and the Dickey-Fuller demeaned median gives `-0.013`, against our measured deficit of
  0.18 to 0.37, i.e. **15x to 35x too small**. And any `O(1/T)` bias must shrink with the record,
  while ours did not move from `nf = 400` to `3700`. Caveat: neither constant is verified against a
  fetched primary source (the same debt as `thread-CD-bias-through-integrator.md` item D3), so cite
  the refutation, not the numbers.
- *Window shorter than system memory.* Ribeiro, Tiels, Umenberger, Schon, Aguirre, *Automatica*
  121:109158, 2020, DOI `10.1016/j.automatica.2020.109158` (**already on disk**), Theorem 1: cost
  smoothness grows `O(L_h^(3N))` for `L_h > 1` but only `O(N^3)` at `L_h = 1`. Horizon is a strong
  lever only outside the marginal region, so `nf = 3700` agreeing with `nf = 400` is the predicted
  behaviour. Two clauses we had never used: non-contractive systems "will always have `L_h >= 1`",
  and the objective is hard to find minima in "**or, even, to escape from them**", which is the
  mirror image of the BLA arm holding what it was given.

**Provable-learnability boundary, worth quoting in the thesis.** Hardt, Ma, Recht, *JMLR*
19(29):1-44, 2018 (arXiv:1609.05191) prove SGD reaches the global optimum only for
`alpha`-acquiescent systems, which "implies (via Rouche's theorem) that the spectral radius of `A`
is smaller than 1", and the assumption additionally **excludes clustered roots**, their own
counterexample being `p(z) = (z - 0.99)^n`. Our plant is two coincident poles at exactly `z = 1`:
the excluded configuration at the excluded radius. Miller and Hardt (ICLR 2019, arXiv:1805.10369)
state the scope limit plainly: "only stable linear dynamical systems are known to be learnable via
gradient descent."

**Why N4SID succeeds, quantified.** Tsiamis and Pappas, CDC 2019, arXiv:1903.09122, Assumption 1 is
`rho(A) <= 1`, "more general than the stricter condition `rho(A) < 1` found in previous works", and
Remark 2 gives the subspace rate as `O(log N / sqrt(N))` for marginally stable systems against
`O(sqrt(log N log log N / N))` when strictly stable. **The marginal case costs one log factor.**
They also note `A - KC` is strictly stable even when `A` is marginal, so the predictor object is
always well conditioned, which is the modern form of the OKID observation. Their Lemma E.2 gives
`sum norm(A^i) = O(t^kappa)` with `kappa` the largest unit-circle Jordan block, so at our
`kappa = 2` an `nf`-step rollout's sensitivity grows like `nf^2`.

**Scope limit found in our own seed, and it matters.** Ramkannan, Beintema, Toth, Schoukens,
*IFAC-PapersOnLine* 56(2):5146-5151, 2023, DOI `10.1016/j.ifacol.2023.10.010`, Section 2 declares
the paper's class to be "**fading memory** nonlinear discrete-time systems". Two poles at `z = 1`
is not fading memory, so **our system is outside the stated model class of the BLA-initialisation
paper we are building on**. Marconato, Sjoberg, Suykens, Schoukens, IEEE T-IM 63(4):972-980, 2014,
DOI `10.1109/tim.2013.2283553`, is the counterweight to read before over-claiming the BLA result:
"in most cases, when starting from a linear model, the optimization routine gets stuck in bad local
minima." Floren and Swevers, IEEE L-CSS 10:745-750, 2026, DOI `10.1109/lcsys.2026.3701764`, is the
2026 frontier position and agrees: on F-16 the "linear-only initialization fails to escape a poor
local minimum".

**Still unexplained, and now sharply so: the `SS_f` sensitivity** (`bla_init.py:50-60`, choice moves
`max abs(eig)` between 0.999995 and 1.41). Chiuso, IEEE TAC 55(3):634-649, 2010, DOI
`10.1109/tac.2009.2039239` (read via TU/e; no free copy exists), Theorem 5.1 makes the asymptotic
**variance** of any system invariant monotonically non-increasing in the future horizon under his
Condition 1, which covers proportional feedback and LQG with white reference. That is variance, not
bias, and gives no mechanism for poles to move; his standing assumption is on the joint spectrum,
giving `max abs(eig(A - KC)) < 1` for the *predictor*, which our plant can satisfy. Bauer,
*Econometrics* 14(1):12, 2026, DOI `10.3390/econometrics14010012` (gold OA), supplies the only
finite-horizon bias argument found ("for `rho = 1` this argument does not work any more"), but for
spectral **zeros** under a stable `A`, not poles. So the `SS_f` observation is unexplained against a
specific theorem rather than unexplained in general.

**Cheap open test, not started.** Whether our flat point at `0.128-0.133` is an optimum or a
plateau. Li, Han, E, Li, ICLR 2021, arXiv:2009.07799, prove an exponentially long escape time from a
long-memory plateau where "adding long-term memories in targets ... increase[s] the loss with little
effect on the gradient and Hessian". An lr sweep cannot distinguish that from an optimum, because on
such a plateau the gradient is genuinely `o(1)` while the loss excess is `O(1)`. Discriminator:
measure the gradient norm along the pole-radius direction against the loss excess.

### BLA injection verified, and a float32 floor found doing it (2026-08-11)

Test criterion taken from the primary source, **Schoukens and Toth, "On the Initialization of
Nonlinear LFR Model Identification with the Best Linear Approximation", arXiv:2004.05040, Sec 4.2**
(on disk at `literature/lpv-lfr/Schoukens_2020_LFRInitializationBLA.pdf`): "the initial model has
the same performance as the BLA estimate since `B_w` and `D_yw` are initialized as zero matrices".
`--bla-zero-nl` is our analogue, so at epoch 0 the network must BE the BLA. `scratchpad/bla_equiv.py`.

**Rungs 1-3 PASS at float32 precision**: one step on 64 random `(x,u)` gives relative `1.54e-07`
on the next state and `8.81e-08` on the output; the initialised network's Jacobian spectrum equals
`eig(A)` to `8.15e-07`; a 400-step rollout from a shared `x0` agrees to `8.26e-06`. **The injection
is correct.**

**Rung 4 (9000-step free run, relative `8.70e-04`) is NOT a bug**, it is float32 round-off
amplified by the double integrator. Two confirmations: running the reference in float32 shrinks the
discrepancy **100x** (`5.05e-05` against `4.89e-03` at N=9000), and the growth is **quadratic in N**
(`1.0 / 15.8 / 1311` against `N^2` predictions `1.0 / 16.0 / 1296`), which is `kappa = 2` for a
size-2 Jordan block at `z = 1`, exactly Tsiamis and Pappas' Lemma E.2. An injection error would be
flat in N.

**NEW LIMIT, and it bears on every score in this campaign: float32 accumulation over the 12 s free
run is `1.436e-04 m` RMS (`2.775e-04` max; per channel `1.514e-04, 1.514e-04, 1.265e-04`) against
the FP baseline bar of `1.883e-04 m`. Numerical round-off alone is 76% of the bar.** In float32 a
model cannot demonstrably reach the FP baseline on this record. **This supersedes
`results/pretrain_diagnostic.json`'s `f32_gap = 5.93e-06` and its "precision is NOT a limit"
conclusion for these arms**: that was the true 8-state model at 4 kHz in physical coordinates, while
this is our model at 800 Hz in the BLA's unit-variance realisation, 24x worse conditioned. Any
future claim of approaching `1.883e-04` needs float64 propagation or a conditioning fix first.

**Deviations from the paper, noted, none yet shown to be errors.** It estimates the BLA with
MATLAB `ssest`, a subspace-initialised **PEM refinement**, where we use N4SID alone with no
refinement; it minimises simulation error with **Levenberg-Marquardt** plus a Jacobian-SVD
coordinate frame, where we use Adam on a windowed loss; and it draws `W_w, W_z, b_z ~ U(-1,1)`
while `--bla-zero-nl` zeroes the nonlinear branches outright. Also: the paper's benchmarks
(Bouc-Wen, parallel Wiener-Hammerstein) are **asymptotically stable**, so like Ramkannan's
"fading memory" scope it says nothing about a plant with poles at `z = 1`. The state unit-variance
transform of its eq. (6) IS applied, by deepSI itself at `ss_linear.py:295-299`, not by us.

### Corrections made this session

- **`epochs: 9999` in every pre-existing metrics file is a decoy.** `fit_system.py:349` lets
  `--n-its` override `epochs`; the July runs were capped at 110-220 updates deliberately.
- **The metrics files were structurally blind past the best epoch**, because `fit()` reloads the
  `_best` checkpoint before returning. Fixed in `ann_blackbox.py`: `full_Loss_val` and friends are
  recovered from the `_last` checkpoint, and `argv` / `n_its` / `timeout` are now recorded.
- **`blafullz`'s `loss_train = [NaN]` was never a crash**, it is the initial validation's
  placeholder under `--n-its 1`.
- **DIAGNOSIS-PROMPT Section 2's "Y is the axis owned by the absorber" does not match `plant.py`.**
  `_K4[2,3] = _C4[2,3] = 0`; Y-to-absorber coupling is inertial only, via `M8[2,3] = MA`.
- **My own error: the MSD morph experiment used noise scaled to the output std**, which on the
  free-floating arm put sigma at 25.3 against vibration content of 0.045, burying the dynamics at
  **-55 dB**. Sensor noise is absolute. Arms B and C as first run are **invalid**; **do not cite
  the first B/C numbers (`0.618`, `0.999`)**.
  **Corrected rerun at Jan's absolute `15e-3`, 2026-08-11: A `epoch0 0.16761 -> 0.028086`, 5.97x,
  `best/mean 0.171`, learned `max abs(lambda) = 0.99716` against a true `0.996232`. B
  `epoch0 989.18 -> 648.13`, only 1.53x, `best/mean 0.622`, learned `max abs(lambda) = 0.96008`
  with ZERO modes above 0.999 against the true TWO.** So the pole-at-1 arm **degrades badly but
  does not collapse**: it learns something (0.622 of its mean predictor, not 0.999), and it moves
  toward the unit circle (0.960, not 0.624). The honest claim is therefore weaker than first
  reported: **adding a free rigid-body mode costs ~4x in improvement factor and prevents the
  network from placing ANY mode above 0.999, but it is a degradation, not a total failure.**
  `max abs(lambda) = 0.960` at `dt = 0.02` is a 0.49 s time constant against a true infinity.
  **Arm C (the pole-versus-offset discriminator) was killed at update 72 and is NOT done**, so the
  attribution between "the pole" and "the train/val offset" is still open.

### Still open

1. **Is the BLA arm converged?** No. Quartile bests `0.0985 / 0.0504 / 0.0369 / 0.0335`, still
   descending. Log-log rate `updates^-0.58` but **R2 = 0.13**, so the extrapolation (3200x this run
   to reach `frozen_lti`) is not trustworthy. A 12 h server run at `--epochs 4000 --timeout 43200`
   settles it.
2. **LinENC (`--bla full`) is defective.** Epoch-0 `0.67728` where it must beat LinDY's `0.31768`;
   final `0.17202`, worse than a constant. Training loss `0.018` at update 280 against validation
   `0.245` points at **time alignment** rather than normalisation. Worth fixing: Jan's own
   `msd_ndof_pre_encoder.py` shows he needed encoder pre-training too, but trains it supervised on
   true states, which a black box cannot use.
3. ~~**WHY gradient descent cannot reach `abs(lambda) = 1` is unexplained.**~~ **ANSWERED by the
   literature sweep, see "The mechanism" above.** Two independent representation failures: the
   rolled-out input-only objective is provably inconsistent at `rho(A) = 1` even noiseless
   (Simchowitz Theorem C.1), and `abs(lambda) = 1` is unreachable at finite parameters in the
   standard parameterisations (Cayley/`exp(-exp(.))` pathology). What remains open is narrower:
   (a) the `SS_f` sensitivity, unexplained against Chiuso's variance theorem; (b) whether our flat
   point is an optimum or Li et al.'s plateau; (c) **arm C of the MSD morph, the pole-versus-offset
   discriminator, killed at update 72**, so that attribution is still open. Arms A and B did finish
   (see Corrections) and corroborate layer B; only `msdff_grounded_abs.json` survives in the session
   scratchpad, so the arm B numbers live in this log rather than in a JSON on disk.
4. **Capacity is the last untested structural difference.** Jan's encoder sees 26 inputs at width
   16; ours sees 102 at width 16, and his `f`/`h` are SISO where ours are 3x3 MIMO with
   Y-dependent inertia, at the same 2x8.

### What this means for the thesis

A black box given every advantage, including a linear initialisation that supplies the correct
pole structure, sits **178x above the untrained FP baseline** and **80x worse than a plain linear
model** even with a fitted initial state. That is the brief's anticipated "converges and is still
far above the baseline" outcome.

**After the literature sweep the argument for augmentation is stronger than a benchmark comparison,
and the word "provably" is now literal rather than rhetorical.** The gantry's marginal modes are
*structural*: `_K4` has zeros on X and Y because there is no stiffness there. In the augmented model
those poles come from the physics block, by construction, at exactly `z = 1`, and the ANN never has
to learn them, so it never meets either failure layer. The black box must recover them from `u` and
`y` alone, and that is the sub-problem the literature says is ill-posed: Simchowitz's Theorem C.1
makes least squares inconsistent at `rho(A) = 1` with the error lower bound *growing* as `N^(k-1)`
even without noise, Hardt-Ma-Recht's learnability guarantee excludes both the radius and the
clustered-root configuration our plant has, and OKID states outright that the truncated
input-output description does not exist for a marginally stable plant.

So the thesis claim upgrades from "augmentation is more sample-efficient and more interpretable" to
**"for this plant class the black box is attacking a provably ill-posed sub-problem, and physics-based
augmentation sidesteps it by construction"**. That is defensible from cited theorems rather than from
our benchmark numbers alone, and it survives a reviewer who suspects our black-box arm was simply
undertrained or badly tuned.

Two honest limits to carry into the write-up. Our seed paper for the BLA initialisation
(Ramkannan 2023) restricts itself to **fading-memory** systems, so we are applying it outside its
stated class and should say so rather than cite it as cover. And Marconato 2014 plus Floren and
Swevers 2026 both report linear initialisation failing to escape poor local minima, so the honest
framing of our BLA result is "necessary here, not sufficient in general".

**Consequence for the work plan, recorded for the decision rather than acted on:** the literature
points at a *parameterisation* fix (a Lie-exponential or free-real-part continuous-time generator, in
which `abs(lambda) = 1` is an interior finite parameter value) rather than an initialisation fix. That
would be a well-motivated study in its own right and is **not** on the thesis critical path, because
the BLA-initialised arm already does the only job the black box has, which is to be an honest
comparison baseline. Logged so the option is visible; not queued.

---

HORIZON, decisively.** Discrimination (trained / planted): **1.25x at 0.1 s, 1.65x at 0.2 s, 2.18x at 0.4 s, 2.54x at 0.8 s**, against 3.34x on the 12 s free run. The two columns separately give the mechanism: the planted model's window error falls **54 %** with length (`1.2068e-06` -> `5.5332e-07`) because its error is TRANSIENT-dominated, while the trained model's is **flat, 6.6 %** (`1.5072e-06` -> `1.4071e-06`) because its error is PERSISTENT. At `nf = 400` both are dominated by the same transient, which is why the loss cannot separate them and why every optimiser in the row above lands in the same basin. **A statistic that misleads and is recorded so it is not repeated:** with a perfect latent init the planted model's ratio to the per-window FLOOR gets WORSE with `nf` (10.20 -> 13.12), which reads as an argument against horizon; it is not, because the floor amortises the same way the planted model does (`xc = 0` at every window start is its whole error). Model-against-model is the correct statistic here. **Consequence: ~0.8 s of objective is worth roughly a doubling of gradient signal, and at 4 kHz that is `nf = 3200` of BPTT depth, i.e. inside the refuted regime.** **CORRECTED SAME DAY (D-148 finding 10): the first reading of this row proposed multiple shooting (`n_seg = 8` x `nf_seg = 400`) as the way to buy that length without the depth. That is WRONG. `multiple_shooting.py` RE-ENCODES at every segment start (`x = x_node` after `x_node = self.encoder(...)`), so eight segments of 400 contain EIGHT transients, one per 400 samples, exactly today's density; the 2.54x came from ONE transient per 3200 samples. It would have returned roughly today's 1.25x, which is consistent with it never having improved anything. The horizon READING of this table stands; the proposed remedy does not, and the actual remedy is the burn-in row below.** |
| **BURN-IN: can the objective be fixed AT nf = 400 by not scoring the startup transient?** (`cl_burnin_sweep.py`, 2026-08-19; no training) | Planted and trained models rescored on the same windows and the same encoder init, objective `V = w_burn*||e over [0,K)||^2 + ||e over [K,nf)||^2`, over `K` in {0,50,100,200}, `w_burn` in {0,0.1} and `W^a` in {random, zero}. Four rollouts; the grid is a re-reduction of the same per-sample error. | 400 | discrimination = trained criterion / planted criterion | n/a | **Hypothesis: the `1/n` scaling found in N1 is a FIXED startup transient, so the fix is to stop SCORING it rather than to dilute it with a longer window.** Decomposition that motivated it: at `nf = 400`, **88 % of the planted model's window loss is transient against 15 % of the trained one's**, and the planted model has 3.9x MORE startup energy because it USES its latent states, so the loss penalises a model for using its augmented states. **Falsifier: if burn-in does not raise the discrimination, the `1/n` reading is wrong and horizon work is back to being primary.** **OUTCOME: the falsifier did not fire, and the result exceeds the horizon route.** Discrimination goes from **1.249x today to 3.312x at `K = 100`** (`W^a` random) and **3.400x** with `W^a = 0`, against **2.54x for an eight-times-longer window** and **3.34x for the 12 s free run**. So discarding the first 100 samples of each window recovers the FULL free-run discrimination at `nf = 400`, with no extra gradient depth, no rate change and no segment-boundary semantics; length dilutes the transient while burn-in removes it. `K = 100` is 25 ms, matching the ~20 ms settling implied by the loop's 100 Hz crossover and 33.8 deg phase margin. **Three consequences: `W^a` stops mattering (1.975x vs 1.249x at `K = 0`, but 3.400x vs 3.312x at `K = 100`), so adopt zero for consistency rather than for performance; and an explicit initialisation term costs real discrimination (`w_burn = 0.1` drops 3.400x to 2.995x), which is the honest price of keeping the encoder explicitly trained.** Proxy caveat: this shows the loss can RANK the right answer, not that training will FIND it. **AND IT IS ONLY HALF THE FIX (D-148 finding 10): burn-in stops grading the ANN on an initialisation error it cannot fix, but it leaves the encoder untrained, i.e. it works AROUND the encoder rather than fixing it. The encoder criterion is the multiple-shooting DEFECT term, `defects.append(x_node - x)`, which penalises encoder-against-dynamics disagreement with gradient into both and needs NO ground truth for the latent coordinate, so it dissolves the gauge problem. The two are complementary: burn-in for the dynamics criterion, defect for the encoder criterion, and neither substitutes for the other.** |

Latent gauge fixed to the true absorber states, which is legitimate for an existence question and NOT for "training will find this one". | 400 | free run and window RMS, both production scorers | n/a | **OUTCOME: the class can represent it, its OUTPUT PARAMETERISATION cannot, and the objective barely ranks the answer.** (1) The eight required corrections span **nine decades**, `3.9e-08` on X to `1.03` on the latents, out of one shared output layer. (2) Two regression arms differing only in parameterisation: as-parameterised today the two latent rows are **unfittable** (`1-R^2` 0.98 and 0.91); with per-row output scaling they fit to `9.6e-05` and `1.5e-04`, and `dTh` and `dX` improve by three orders. (3) The planted model scores **`4.177e-07` m free run**, i.e. **82 % of the headroom closed against training's 36.7 %**, and `4.122e-07` from the true `x0`. **DIAGNOSTIC CEILING ONLY: built from `x_aug`, must never become a deliverable init.** (4) On the window metric the same model is only **1.26x** better than the trained one (1.99x with `W^a = 0`) while the free run rates it 3.3x better; even with the TRUE latent at every window start it reaches only `7.160e-07` against `4.122e-07` free-run, so 41 % of the window penalty is latent initialisation and the remaining 1.7x is horizon. (5) `cl_band_split.py` on the trained model: the improvement is **entirely in the excited band** (`+37.4 %` at 130-180 Hz, still **49.3x** the oracle there) while the motion band gets **3.9x worse** (402x the oracle) and the gap band 1.4x worse. That damage costs only **0.5 percentage points** of the total improvement (36.78 % counterfactual against 36.28 % actual), which is exactly why the optimiser buys it: the 150 Hz peak is two to three decades above everything else, so what the loss cannot see, the optimiser spends. |

So LinENC is the black-box-legal version of a trick Jan needed too, and it is worth FIXING rather than abandoning.** |

Jan's MSD has no such shift, which is why his converges and this does not. **Gate, set before launch: `rms_Y` must fall below `0.0448`, the val Y signal std, or the model still has not learned Y at all. Falsifier: if `rms_Y` stays near `0.20` with 14 records spanning Y = -30..+30, Y coverage is NOT the blocker and the failure is structural in the model class, which is the brief's own "valid and important outcome".** **Outcome (2026-08-11, stopped at 2008 updates / 4 epochs once the gate was decided): GATE FAILED, FALSIFIER FIRED, and the mechanism is now named.** `rms_Y = 0.2472` against the gate of `0.0448`, and it moved the **wrong way** (single-record `0.2092`), because the pooled mean over Y = -30..+30 sits further from `V2`'s Y than `T10`'s mean did. The model's Y output has mean `-0.0101` against a true `-0.2339`: it parks at the training-set mean. **Per the log's own drift-is-not-offset rule this is an OFFSET, not drift:** a linear fit to the Y error gives `+1.5 mm/s` with **R2 = 0.002**, i.e. no trend at all. **Mechanism, from the Jacobian of the learned state map at five points along `V2`: max |lambda| = 0.75 (14-record) and 0.92-1.01 (single-record), so the slowest learned mode has tau = 4 ms and 35 ms respectively, against the true system's TWO POLES AT z = 1 (tau = infinity).** The learned model is strongly contracting and has no integrator, so a free run forgets its initial condition in milliseconds and relaxes to the data mean; it physically cannot hold a position. More data made this WORSE, not better, pushing max |lambda| from 0.98 down to 0.75. **Why the objective permits it: the encoder re-initialises the state every `nf = 400` samples, so the training loss never requires the model to hold state for more than 0.5 s, and a contracting input-driven filter scores as well there as a marginally stable one. The free integrators are invisible to a windowed loss and are therefore never learned.** This is the brief's "converges and is still far above the baseline" outcome: the failure is structural, and the next question is the model class, not the training. It is also a direct argument FOR the thesis's augmentation route, the physics baseline supplying exactly the marginal rigid-body dynamics the black box provably will not learn. |
| **lr SWEEP: is the update-140 reversal a step-size instability or structural?** (`scratchpad/objective_split.py --lr {1e-3,3e-4,1e-4} --n-its 440`, 2026-08-11, row written BEFORE launch) | Same shipped `nf400` gantry arm and same untouched deepSI loop as the objective-split row below, seed 0 throughout, so the three arms differ **only** in learning rate. lr is set through `init_model(optimizer_kwargs=dict(lr=...))`, the only place it can be set once `init_model` has run (D-101); `fit` is never given an lr. Budget doubled to **440 updates** because three points past the minimum cannot separate a reversal from a transient Adam excursion, and a recovery would change the reading. | 400 | free-run sim-RMS and windowed `nf=400`, both records, at all 13 validation points | 440 updates per arm (12 validations + initial) | **Hypothesis: the reversal at update 140 is an Adam step-size instability.** **Discriminator, decided before launch:** if the turnaround moves later as lr falls, it is step size and the fix is in the optimiser; if it stays pinned near update 140 at every lr, step size is not the mechanism and the marginal poles at `z=1` (D-134) become the prime suspect; if it merely scales proportionally with lr, the run accumulates per-update rather than per-unit-time. **Falsifier for the whole framing: if the 1e-3 control arm does NOT reproduce the reversal at 440 updates, the three-point turnaround was noise and this row is void.** **Outcome (2026-08-11): THE FALSIFIER FIRED. The reversal is a transient, and the real behaviour is a plateau.** At 440 updates the 1e-3 arm recovers, `free_val` going `0.1289` (140) -> `0.1461` (210) -> `0.1299` (245) and then oscillating in `0.129-0.140` with no trend, while `wind_train` ends at `0.6241`, **below** its update-140 value of `0.6467`. So the optimiser is not walking away from its objective; the excursion at 175-210 was noise on three points, exactly the caveat flagged when it was reported. What all three arms actually show: `free_val` falls fast and then **flattens at 0.128-0.133 regardless of lr** (1e-3 flat from update 140, 3e-4 flat from 245, 1e-4 still descending at 420 and merely slower, `0.1473`), while `wind_train` keeps creeping down. Against `mean_pred = 0.147` on this record, **0.13 is 0.90x, i.e. the converged model is 10% better than predicting a constant**, and it reaches that in about 140 updates. **Conclusion: the black box is neither undertrained nor unstable. It converges quickly to a useless solution, which makes the failure structural (model class, objective, or data), not a training-budget or optimiser problem.** This retires the entire "train it longer" family, including the brief's own leading hypothesis. |
| **OBJECTIVE-SPLIT 2x2: is the turnaround overfitting or a train/validate objective mismatch? NEITHER** (`scratchpad/objective_split.py`, 2026-08-11, row written before launch) | Shipped `nf400` gantry arm, seed 0, `--n-its 220`, Adam default lr 1e-3, construction identical to the one Part 0 proved bit-identical to Jan's. The deepSI training loop is **untouched**: `cal_validation_error` is shadowed on the instance so `fit()` still selects checkpoints on exactly the same number, while each call additionally records free-run sim-RMS on the **train** record and the windowed `nf=400` loss on a fixed seeded 256-window subset of **both** records. | 400 | free-run sim-RMS **and** windowed `nf=400`, each on both records | 220 updates (6 validations + initial) | **Hypothesis: free-run val rises after update 140 while windowed train stays flat, so either (a) overfitting, in which case only the val numbers degrade, or (b) an objective mismatch, in which case free-run degrades on both records while windowed improves on both.** **BOTH REFUTED. All four quantities worsen after update 140**, free-run val `+13.3%`, free-run train `+2.8%`, windowed val `+13.8%`, and **windowed train `+33.7%`**, the last being the very quantity Adam is minimising, evaluated at fixed parameters on a fixed batch. Monotone descent on all four up to update 140, then simultaneous reversal. So this is neither generalisation nor metric choice: **the optimiser is making its own training objective worse.** Caveats: three points after the minimum, and the deepSI per-epoch running mean (`0.680` at update 210) is lower than the fixed-parameter snapshot (`0.864`), which locates the rise late inside that epoch rather than spread across it. Next discriminator: whether it recovers over a longer run, and whether lr (via `init_model`'s `optimizer_kwargs`, never `fit`, per D-101) moves the turnaround point. **SUPERSEDED 2026-08-11 by the lr-sweep row above: it does recover, so "the optimiser is making its own training objective worse" is WRONG and must not be quoted. The 2x2 measurement stands and its real content is that all four quantities stop improving together at about update 140; the rise over the next 70 updates was a transient on three points.** |
| **PART 1 TRUNCATION CAUSE, and the metrics files hide the turnaround** (`--fs 800 --nf 400 --n-its 220 --seed 0 --epochs 9999` re-run verbatim into a scratch `--out`, 2026-08-11; read-only re-read of the four stored `results/ann_blackbox_*` artefacts) | Identical to the `fs800_nf400` arm below, seed 0. No new config: this run exists to reproduce a stored artefact and read the bookkeeping arrays (`batch_id`, `epoch_id`, `time`) that the metrics JSON does not carry. | 400 | sim-RMS free run | `--n-its 220`, which per `fit_system.py:349` **overrides `--epochs` entirely**, so the `epochs: 9999` in every metrics JSON was never in force | **Hypothesis: the 5-to-6 validation points are a silent truncation.** **Refuted, twice over.** (1) The caps were deliberate, set per launch in the 2026-07-31 session and recoverable only from the session transcript, there being no shell-history record: `nf400` and `nf3700` both `--n-its 220`, `blafullz` `--n-its 1`, `bladynz` `--timeout 3600`. The one long run, `--nf 3700 --epochs 400 --timeout 21600`, was manually stopped at 56 its / 3m27s, so **no long gantry run has ever completed**. (2) **The `nf400` run did execute all 220 updates.** It validated six times, at 35/70/105/140/175/210, giving `0.1499, 0.1402, 0.1322, 0.1289, 0.1362, 0.1461`: the free-run validation error **bottoms at update 140 and then rises 13% over the next 70 updates while the train loss stays flat at 0.82**. The stored artefact holds only the first four because `fit()` reloads the `_best` checkpoint before returning (`fit_system.py:501`) and `ann_blackbox.py` reads `Loss_val` off the reloaded object, so **the metrics JSON is structurally incapable of showing anything after the best epoch.** That is why `best_epoch` equals the last `epoch_id` in all four artefacts and why `n_val` is small. **Consequence: the claim that `loss_val` decreases monotonically and never flattens is an artefact of this mechanism, and the "undertrained by three orders of magnitude" reading is wrong: at 140 updates the gantry black box is already past its optimum.** `blafullz`'s `loss_train=[NaN]` is likewise not a defect, it is the `float('nan')` placeholder of the initial validation at `fit_system.py:367`, the only validation `--n-its 1` permits. |

T4 exists to remove exactly that confound by regenerating the data. But that row's *other* finding is not removed by T4 and sharpens the prediction: the position-mode decay rate is **damping-limited**, -c/(2m) = -0.323 rad/s, and is INDEPENDENT of k above k = c^2/(4m) (5.63 N/m for X). Consequences, and they are the falsifier: (a) within a 400-sample (0.1 s) training window the spring decays error by only ~3%, so **the training loss should come out nearly identical to `t1_nf400`**; (b) over the 12 s free run tau = 3.09 s gives ~50x decay, so **val sim-RMS is where the effect must appear if it appears at all**. Same-sign across independent inits = systematic (diffusion would scatter). **(B) mean dLoss/d(bias) over the epoch, 3 seeds, reproduces in sign+magnitude AND matches the DC direction on the dominant rows:** dY +2.15/+2.47/+2.70e-5 (pushes dY negative = the observed DC), Y -2.06/-1.48/-1.82e-4 (pushes Y positive = observed); dX is the lone mismatch (grad>0 but DC>0, both tiny). Per-seed |t|<1 (loss nearly flat in this direction, minibatch-noisy) but 3-seed replication is decisive â€” per-seed variance test is underpowered exactly as the lit predicts (GSNR/grad-noise-scale degenerate at full-batch determinism; cross-seed agreement is the instrument). **CONCLUSION:** the windowed loss is nearly NEUTRAL to the DC (flat loss, weak gradient) but carries a small CONSISTENT bias that the K=0 integrators accumulate into the drift = a systematic loss-geometry cause (G-C), NOT random wander. Ties d12/f09 loss-blindness + Rubruck (mean/constant learned first) + Tallec-Ollivier (truncated-BPTT under-prices the slow direction). Fix implication: neither better init nor input design removes it -> the robust fix is a direct SOFT PIN on the DC direction (zero-at-equilibrium). Prediction: at matched lr, SGD builds far less DC than Adam AT THE SAME LOSS. **Outcome (2026-07-18, seed 0): CONFIRMED -- ADAM IS THE AMPLIFIER.** SGD dY DC = **+1.98e-9** at step 250 vs the Adam control **-3.98e-6** (~2000x smaller), at the SAME loss (~1.5-2e-6, so SGD is training, not stalled). This CLOSES the mechanism: every source refuted (physics v1f, encoder-init v3x0, excitation broadband, normalization, encoder), the loss is flat in the DC direction, and Adam walks the ANN off the zero-mean center into that flat valley (SGD does not). Fix options (consistent): soft DC pin (any optimizer), a non-adaptive optimizer (SGD avoided it here at no loss cost), or an Adam variant without flat-direction creep. 3-seed confirm running (seeds 1,2). |

Two modes, identical everything except the loss: `fixed` = standard nf=400 window (reproduces v3b, dY DC ~-4e-6) = CONTROL; `artbp` = random horizon K ~ geometric(q=1/nf) capped at H_max=1600 (MEAN horizon = nf=400 so matched average cost), per-step loss reweighted by w_k=(1-q)^{-(k-1)} (Tallec-Ollivier reweighting, forward-window adaptation) so the gradient is unbiased for the long-horizon loss. | mean 400 (artbp) / 400 (fixed) | per-step DC (mean ann(Zpts) dY) + loss | 1 ep x 3 seeds | **Hypothesis (closes "what causes the drive"): the systematic DC-gradient is the finite-window (truncated-BPTT) bias; an UNBIASED gradient at the SAME average cost removes it.** ARTBP differs from longer fixed windows (already refuted: DC~1/nf, nonzero at every nf up to 3200) by attacking the BIAS directly at fixed mean horizon. Pre-declared: `fixed` reproduces -4e-6 sign-locked (validates the harness); `artbp` DC collapses toward the ~1e-7 noise floor / loses its reproducible sign -> the drive WAS the truncation bias (mechanism confirmed by intervention) AND ARTBP is a candidate fix at the training horizon. Falsification: artbp DC ~ -4e-6 unchanged -> the drive is NOT truncation bias, look elsewhere. WATCH: ARTBP is high-variance (rare long windows x growing weights); if training destabilizes, reduce H_max. **Outcome (2026-07-22, 3 seeds each): FULL PASS -- the drive IS the truncated-BPTT bias, confirmed by intervention.** fixed control dY DC = **-4.92 / -4.56 / -4.39e-6, ALL sign-locked negative** (reproduces v3b, validates the harness). ARTBP dY DC = **-5.24e-8 / +2.64e-7 / +2.71e-7**: magnitude collapses ~20-90x into the +/-3.4e-7 clean-noise band AND the SIGN SCATTERS (-,+,+) vs the control's locked negative. So an unbiased gradient at the SAME average horizon (nf=400) removes both the magnitude and the reproducible sign of the DC -> the systematic drive was the finite-window truncation bias (Tallec-Ollivier), not data/physics/code. Distinct from longer FIXED windows (refuted: DC~1/nf, nonzero to nf=3200); ARTBP attacks the bias directly at fixed cost. **Dual result:** (1) closes "what causes the drive" by intervention (mechanism proven); (2) ARTBP is a CANDIDATE FIX at the affordable training horizon (unlike longer windows). One bug fixed mid-run: K=1 windows have only the detached encoder-init output (no gradient) -> require K>=2 (P(K=1)=0.25%, negligible). Data: `v12_{fixed,artbp}_seed*.npz`, logs `v12_{fixed,artbp}_run.log`. |

Measures the DC-direction on the dY route (state idx 5) as (a) the raw autograd gradient `d(mean-MSE)/dc` at c=0 [ground_truth.py], and (b) the loss landscape `L(c)` over a 13-pt c-grid -> parabola fit `c*` (loss-optimal constant), `kappa` (curvature), bootstrap SE over windows [instrument_select.py, Phase B0]. H sweep {50..3200}(grad), {400,1600,3200}(landscape); planted-offset positive control eps={4e-6,1e-6,3e-7}; v11 cross = true-init + raw dY-state-row. | up to 3200 | DC-direction gradient / c*(H) / kappa(H) / injection recovery | n/a (no training) | **Hypothesis (does the Phase B plan's `true_grad(T)` ground-truth + 1/nf-bias framing hold, and is the loss-landscape a better + faithful instrument?):** Phase B pre-declared a convergent long-horizon gradient and a ~1/nf bias gap. Pre-registered B0 tests: (1) landscape rel-SE << raw-grad and bounded in H; (2) recover a planted -eps; (3) does `c*(H)` match the v12 trained DC (-4.5e-6)? **Outcome: FULL RESULT, framing REVISED (D-120).** (a) The raw gradient does NOT converge on the z=1 axis: variance explodes ~H^3, mean unresolvable (SE~=mean, |t|<1.3 at every H) -> **no `true_grad(T)` exists**; the "1/nf bias gap" framing is void. (b) Landscape: **Test 1** kappa rel-SE <0.05% vs raw-grad ~42% (~1000x better); `g = -kappa*c*` held to 3 sig figs with kappa 3.1e2->6.9e4->9.1e5 (~H^3.8) and c* +1.70e-6->+4.4e-7->+1.95e-7 (->0). **Test 2** planted eps recovered 99.8-100.1% (SE~1e-9), beats v8-inj (64% at 3e-7). **Test 3** loss optimum is small + convention-dependent (`c*(400)`=+1.7e-6 ann-route, -2.44e-6 v11 state-row), ->0 with horizon, NEVER the trained -4.5e-6. **Conclusion:** the DC is Adam walking a near-flat, weakly-constrained direction (small kappa at nf=400), NOT a loss optimum; kappa(H) growing is the restoring curvature ARTBP supplies via rare long rollouts (reconciles v3b systematic-gradient vs v11/SGD flat-direction, explains v12 ARTBP collapse). **Reframe:** adopt kappa(H) as the mechanism instrument; verify the FIX by the trained DC trajectory {fixed, ARTBP-geometric, ARTBP-poly-tail}, not gradient unbiasedness. Data: `ARTBP/data/{b_bias_gap,b0_instrument_select}.npz`, figs `ARTBP/figures/{b_bias_gap,b0_landscapes,b0_instrument}.png`. |
| **ARTBP Phase D VARIANCE GRID (poly-tail vs geometric), `ARTBP/train_artbp.py` + `runners/run_phase_d_grid.sh`** (2026-07-22) | Real with-MSD augmentation data, encoder init, Adam lr=1e-7, BATCH=256, stride=10, routing 0..7, nf=400, H_max=1600, 1 epoch, 20% held-out windows for the fit gate. 4 modes x 5 seeds (SLURM array 0-19, one (mode,seed)/task): `fixed` (biased control), `geom` (v12 geometric, exp weights), `poly4`/`poly6` (Eq.14 poly-tail alpha=4/6, poly weights). Per-step instrumentation: mean ann(Zpts) dY (DC), dLoss/d(bias) dY via patched ann.forward (v3b probe; its 2nd-half variance = the DC-direction estimator variance = primary metric), sampled K, max weight. | 400 (mean) / 1600 cap | endpoint DC dY + dcgrad variance + held-out nf-RMS | 1 ep x 5 seeds x 4 modes | **Hypothesis (D-120 reframe): both poly-tails collapse the dY DC as well as geometric (reproduce v12) AND reduce the DC-direction estimator variance vs geometric at matched mean horizon (K_bar=nf=400, cap 1600) with held-out nf-RMS not worse than the fixed control; which alpha wins is open.** Pre-registered: (1) fixed DC ~-4.5e-6 sign-locked; (2) geom/poly collapse to <1e-6, sign scattered; (3) Var(poly)/Var(geom) < 1; (4) fit within +10% of fixed. Falsify: Var(poly) >= Var(geom), or poly fails to collapse, or fit degrades. Caveat: z=1 axis lacks the geometric memory decay the paper's variance bound needs -> boundedness not guaranteed, measure the ratio. **1-SEED PROBE (local, 2026-07-22, before the grid): all four criteria look good.** fixed DC -4.49e-6 (locked, var 1.98e-7 baseline); geom DC -5.29e-7, var 21.1, nf-RMS 1.217e-3; poly4 DC -6.11e-7, var 0.896 (24x < geom), nf-RMS 1.170e-3; poly6 DC -7.04e-7, var 0.454 (47x < geom), nf-RMS 1.148e-3 (best). Both poly-tails cut variance 24-47x AND fit better than geom; poly6 (a=6, lighter tail) edges poly4 but the ~2x gap is within single-seed heavy-tail noise -> the 5-seed grid resolves the alpha winner. Residual ARTBP DC (~5-7e-7) matches B0's c*(1600)=+4.4e-7 (the effective-horizon optimum), sign scattered as v12. **Outcome (2026-07-22, 5-seed cluster grid): ALL FOUR CRITERIA PASS; poly6 (alpha=6) is the winner.** fixed DC -4.12e-6 (frac<0 1.00, sign-locked, var 2.05e-7); geom DC -4.03e-7 (frac<0 0.80, var 10.2); poly4 DC -2.58e-7 (frac<0 0.60, var 4.80 = 2.1x<geom); poly6 DC -1.21e-7 (frac<0 0.60, var 2.22 = 4.6x<geom, smallest residual DC). All ARTBP |DC|<5e-7, scattered sign, in B0's c*(1600) band. Fit all +2.1-2.7% vs fixed (PASS, but this is the weak 1-epoch near-baseline fit; gate-2 tests the converged fit). **KEY CORRECTION vs the 1-seed probe (geom 21.1 / poly4 0.90 / poly6 0.45): the true variance reduction is ~2-5x, NOT 24-47x** -- the 1-seed poly numbers were optimistic "quiet" draws (no cap-hit spike in that 2nd-half window); the 5-seed means are higher with sd~=mean (heavy-tailed variance-of-the-variance), vindicating the 5-seed choice (a 1-3 seed estimate oversells the benefit). Ordering (poly6<poly4<geom) robust; poly6-vs-poly4 is ~1sigma (SE~1.3-1.8) so not decisively separated, but poly6 preferred (lower variance AND smallest DC). Data: `ARTBP/data/train_{mode}_seed{0..4}.npz`, fig `ARTBP/figures/phase_d_comparison.png`. Next: gate-2 (converged-fit at production 20 epochs) with poly6, via `runners/run_gate2.sh`. |
| **ARTBP-vs-Y-DESTABILIZATION feedback test (kappa_g(H), no training), `ARTBP/feedback_instrument.py`** (2026-07-22) | Extends the B0 instrument from a CONSTANT to a FEEDBACK GAIN on the Y-route: the ANN Y-output col 5 gets an extra term g*phi(x), phi=dY (anti-damping: force +g*dY opposes the tiny physical damping -> g>0 destabilizes). Faithful (patched ann.forward reads the live state), encoder init, real data, NO training. Sweep L(g) over g in +/-1e-3 (13 pts) at H in {400,1600,3200,6400,12800} (to 3.2 s), calibration first at H=6400. Readouts: DL+/DL- (destabilizing/stabilizing relative loss at g=+/-1.7e-4), effective kappa_g(H) (near-zero parabola over +/-5e-4), div-frac. | up to 12800 (3.2 s) | kappa_g(H), DL+(H), divergence fraction | n/a (no training) | **Hypothesis (whether ARTBP could suppress the second drift component, the state-dependent Y destabilization v5/v6/D-117, in the kappa(H) framework): does the loss penalize a Y anti-damping feedback increasingly with horizon, and at what horizon does it turn on vs the ARTBP cap (1600=0.4 s)? Within reach -> estimator/ARTBP route viable; only at seconds-scale -> favors the D-117 structural (passivity) route.** Pre-registered prediction (mine, a-priori): kappa_g ~0 until the seconds-scale e-folding time then a sharp turn-on -> would need a much larger cap; variance far worse (divergent long rollouts). **Outcome (2026-07-22): PREDICTION CORRECTED BY THE DATA -- turn-on is at the DC's horizon, NOT seconds.** kappa_g(H) = 315 / 4.66e4 / 5.64e5 / 7.27e6 / 1.85e8 at H=400/1600/3200/6400/12800 (~H^3.7, steady, NO saturation) -- essentially IDENTICAL to the DC's kappa(H) (314/6.9e4/9.1e5). The loss landscape along g is a WELL (both damping-error signs raise the loss), ~symmetric at small gain (g=+/-1e-5 -> 2.6x each) and increasingly ASYMMETRIC (destabilizing worse) with gain and horizon (calib H=6400: g=+1e-4 -> 217x vs g=-1e-4 -> 124x; g=+1e-3 -> 2.5e6x vs g=-1e-3 -> 2432x); true DIVERGENCE only at g>=1e-2 (100% runaway), none at the +/-1e-3 fine grid (div-frac 0% at all H). Verdict: the destabilizing response turns on at H~400 (weak kappa_g=315 there -> fixed training under-penalizes it, Adam walks the gain like the DC) and is strong by H_max=1600 (kappa_g=4.66e4) -> **ARTBP at the EXISTING cap should suppress the Y anti-damping feedback by the SAME mechanism and cap it collapses the DC** (unifies both drift components as truncated-training under-penalizing a horizon-growing loss effect on the z=1 mode). This REFUTES the a-priori "needs seconds-cap / worse variance" prediction (no divergence at the operating gain; smooth growing well). **Caveats:** (1) FAITHFULNESS -- this is a SYNTHETIC canonical dY-anti-damping gain on the BASELINE, not the trained ANN's actual v5/v6 direction; if the real destabilization lives in a loss-unpinned direction this test does not capture it. (2) kappa_g is an EFFECTIVE curvature over the +/-5e-4 fit range (its H-growth is robust; the absolute value is fit-range-dependent since L(g) is a steep wall, not locally quadratic over that range). **Decisive close = the direct test:** ARTBP-train the augmentation, then measure the long-horizon Y free-run drift (v5/v6 full/off tail-RMS over ~2 s) vs the fixed control. Relation to D-117: preliminary evidence the ESTIMATOR route (ARTBP) MAY reach the Y destabilization at the existing cap (was leaning structural-necessary); the direct test decides estimator-vs-passivity. Data: `ARTBP/data/b0fb_feedback.npz`, fig `ARTBP/figures/b0fb_feedback.png`. |

| **BASELINE-NULL (baseline-on-baseline, ANN artifact isolation), `baseline-null/run_baseline_null.py`** (2026-07-23) | 6-state PURE baseline (nx_ann=0, ANN routed to physical states 0..5), encoder FROZEN at linear-map init, **orth OFF (orth_beta=0, orth_observe=False -- no joint estimation)**, Adam lr=1e-7, fixed nf=400 windows, STRIDE=100, on BASELINE-ONLY data (USE_MSD=false, `trajectory/augmentation/baseline`, T1-T4,T6-T12 train / T5 held-out free-run). No absorber in the truth -> nothing to learn. Two arms, same model: A = ANN off (frozen zero-init); B = ANN trained. Free-run T5 (~2 s) each epoch capturing per-physical-state trajectory + sim-RMS + tail-drift; decomposition combined(B)/model(A)/ANN(B-A). | nf 400 / free-run 8000 (2 s) | free-run sim-RMS [m] + per-state tail-drift + ANN output | 6 ep x seed 0 | **Hypothesis (user null test): with a perfect baseline and NO signal to fit (baseline data has no absorber), does training the ANN still MANUFACTURE loss + Y/X drift?** Predict: arm A stays at the ~0 floor (flat states); arm B's free-run sim-RMS rises from ~0 and physical states (Y, dY, X) drift away from truth even though the ideal ANN output is 0 -> proves the DC/drift is a pure estimator artifact (truncation-bias + Adam-walk), decoupled from data. Falsify: arm B stays at the floor (ANN stays ~0) -> the drift needs a real signal to form, look elsewhere. **Outcome (2026-07-23, seed 0, 6 ep, build 4s): CONFIRMED -- the ANN manufactures drift with NO signal to fit.** Arm A (ANN off) free-run sim-RMS = 1.58e-5 m (the floor, FLAT, max|ANN|=0). Arm B (ANN trained) sim-RMS rises off the floor: 1.58e-5 (ep0) -> 4.99e-5 (ep1) -> 1.17e-4 (ep2) -> ~1.2-1.4e-4 (ep3-6) = **~8x the floor**. Free-run tail-drift grows on the K=0 translational integrators: Y 3.0e-5 -> 3.65e-4 (~12x), dY 6.2e-6 -> 3.45e-4 (~55x), X to ~1e-4; Theta/dTheta stay put (~0). ANN output grows 0 -> 1.23e-7. DECOMPOSITION (combined = arm B / model = arm A / ANN = B-A): the ANN's own contribution is ~0 on Theta/dX/dTheta but a growing RAMP on X (+) and dY (-) -- a manufactured DC on dY + X drift, integrating into the K=0 axes exactly as the DC/estimator-artifact story predicts. So the DC/drift is a PURE ESTIMATOR ARTIFACT (truncation-bias + Adam-walk), decoupled from any signal (baseline data has no absorber). Magnitudes are ~30x smaller than the real-data-trained DC (baseline gives the ANN less to grab), but clearly nonzero + growing. CAVEATS: single seed, epoch-to-epoch drift non-monotone (free-run drift on one 2s standstill is variable); the decomposition Y panel is dominated by the +0.3 m absolute offset (needs a deviation-from-truth replot). Figures `baseline-null/figures/null_{states_traj,decomposition,development}.png`, data `baseline_null.npz`. Follow-ups: more seeds; orth-ON (beta>0) to test if the projection regularizer suppresses the manufactured DC. **UPDATE (2026-07-23, discriminator run `baseline_null_artbp.npz`): two corrections to the above.** (1) **Model is NOT buggy (decisive):** the baseline TRUE-x0 free-run floor = **3.79e-7 m** = param_recovery's e-07 (param_recovery free-runs from `state_traj[:1]`=true x0). The 1.58e-5 arm-A floor was 97.6% SUBNET encoder-init error (gap +1.54e-5), which cancels in arm A vs B. (2) **Walk back "pure artifact / decoupled from any signal":** on no-signal baseline data fixed training manufactures dY-DC only **-1.09e-7**, ~40x smaller than the real-data DC -4.5e-6. So the LARGE real DC is signal-DEPENDENT (the estimator's biased RESPONSE to the absorber mismatch); the null isolates only the small signal-independent floor. **ARTBP DISCRIMINATOR FAILED to give a clean bug-vs-artifact answer:** ARTBP made it WORSE (dY-DC -1.09e-7->-2.82e-7, sim-RMS 1.24e-4->3.0e-4, noisy ep2 spike 1.3e-3) -- with no signal-driven bias to remove, ARTBP's unbounded variance on the z=1 mode (D-120 caveat) dominates. The TRUE-x0 floor, not ARTBP, settled the bug question. Gradient-sign check inconclusive (dY -6.4e-6 +/- 1.5e-5, not systematic). Corrected conclusion: model correct (e-07); training manufactures drift (estimator artifact); but the big DC is signal-driven, not signal-decoupled. **HORIZON FLOOR (`floor_horizon.py`, data `floor_horizon.npz`, fig `floor_horizon.png`; no training):** baseline (ANN off) free-run floor on T5, true-x0 vs encoder-x0, at 2/4/8/12 s -- true-x0 **3.79e-7 / 6.79e-7 / 3.38e-6 / 4.81e-6 m** (model+discretization floor; grows ~13x with horizon = K=0 marginal-mode accumulation, Y drifts ~12 um over 12 s even from true x0, but stays e-06); encoder-x0 **1.49e-5 / 1.96e-5 / 2.13e-5 / 2.16e-5 m** (nearly flat). Encoder-init gap ~1.7e-5 m, roughly HORIZON-INDEPENDENT -> the e-05 free-run floor is the SUBNET encoder-init error at ALL horizons (2 s and 12 s), NOT a model bug; the model itself is e-07 (2 s) to e-06 (12 s). Note true-x0 = param_recovery's init (`state_traj[:1]`); param_recovery is a different pipeline, so its e-07 is a plausibility cross-check only, not a rigorous identity -- the model-correctness claim rests on the INTERNAL true-x0 floor being 40x below the encoder floor. PER-CHANNEL true-x0 RMSE at 2 s: X1 7.64e-8, X2 7.61e-8, **Y 6.47e-7** (total 3.79e-7 is Y-dominated) -- CONSISTENT with the user's recollection of param_recovery (highest channel e-07, others e-08) but NOT independently verified: the logged synthetic param_recovery is a RECOVERY (detuned init, 600 ep) converging to val_rmse ~4.4e-5 (recovery residual, e-05), NOT a perfect fit; param_recovery's true-params perfect-fit floor was not re-run. Only the Y (K=0 marginal) channel accumulates with horizon (6.47e-7 -> 8.33e-6 over 2->12 s); X1/X2 stay ~1.4e-7. **TRUE-INIT + LR-SWEEP (2026-07-24, `run_baseline_null.py BN_INIT=true`, `lr_sweep.py`):** "does a perfect model with no noise stay at zero?" -> NO, but the mechanism is NOT learning. true-init (remove encoder) drops the drift ~40x (encoder-init was the dominant driver in the encoder run). FULL-BATCH true-init is a transient (ep1 spike then decay, ANN 1.3e-7->1.7e-8), and the WINDOWED loss at ANN=0 is 1.3e-12 and rises under training -> the ANN is DISPLACED from a minimum, not fitting. ONE-STEP lr sweep settles it: ANN offset = **exactly 3.48*lr**, free-run drift **proportional to lr**, -> 3.79e-7 floor at lr=0. So the drift is **step-size-scaled displacement amplified ~1000x by the K=0 integrator, OPTIMIZER-AGNOSTIC** (Adam vs SGD only sets effective lr; supervisor's "Adam=SGD w/ diff lr" holds). CURE structural (constrain K=0 output, D-117), not optimizer/lr. Corrected two over-reads this thread (ep0-6 "systematic march"; "over-fitting the discretization floor"). Figs `lr_sweep.png`, `baseline_null_trueinit{,_fb}.npz`; see `baseline-null/README.md`. |
| **CURVATURE + SENSITIVITY (D5 curvature / D2 sensitivity), `baseline-null/curvature_sensitivity.py`** (2026-07-24) | Companion to BASELINE-NULL. 6-state pure baseline (nx_ann=0, route 0..5), TRUE-init (no encoder in loss/free-run), noiseless baseline data, FULL deterministic bank (5236 windows, no sampling noise), float64. Probe = constant bias b_j on ANN output col j (at zero-init the ANN output IS its final-layer bias, so b_j = unit vector on `final_layer.bias[j]` in real param space). D5: gradient dL/db and Hessian diagonal d2L/db2 at b=0 by FD (eps-swept) AND exact autograd double-backward on the real final-layer bias. SECONDARY = the full-parameter Hessian projection onto this direction (autograd) cross-checking the FD probe. D2: sweep b_j in {0,+-1e-8..+-1e-5}, windowed loss vs free-run tail-drift per channel. NO training (measures loss geometry). | nf 400 / free-run 8000 | curvature d2L/db2 + blindness ratio dDrift/dLoss | seed 0 (no training) | **Hypothesis (mine + `diagnostics-literature.md` D2/D5): the windowed loss is BLIND/FLAT on the marginal (K=0) integrator output-DC (X/Y), so 0 is a non-restoring valley the optimizer walks -> drift; predict tiny curvature on X/Y vs the Theta controls.** **Outcome (2026-07-24): REFUTED. The windowed loss is STIFFEST, not flattest, on the integrator output-DC.** Curvature d2L/db2: X 7.084e4, Y 3.542e4, dX 3.671e3, dY 3.285e2 vs Theta 3.967e-1, dTheta 8.913e-2 (integrator channels ~1e5x the controls, the two positions X/Y the stiffest). VERIFIED rock-solid: (1) EPS-INVARIANT (identical at eps 1e-6/1e-7/1e-8 -> b->output->loss is affine so L(b) is EXACTLY quadratic, curvature is a constant not an estimate); (2) AUTOGRAD == FD EXACTLY (full-param Hessian on the real final-layer bias matches the probe to 4 sig figs on every channel); (3) POSITIVE-DEFINITE Hessian (eig 1.95e-2, 4.48e-1, 1.99e1, 2.15e2, 3.57e4, 7.43e4) -> b=0 is a STRICT local min in the 6-D output-DC subspace; (4) the sloppiest DC eigen-direction is dTheta (a NON-drifting control), not X/Y. D2 (X row): at b=1e-6 the windowed loss rises 6.79e4x (rel L0=4.0e-15) while the free-run drift rises only 2.02e3x -> blindness |dDrift/dLoss| = 3.0e-2 << 1, the loss OVER-constrains the DC. **MECHANISM (why not blind):** the 400-step window CONTAINS the same integrator, so a constant DC RAMPS within-window and the summed MSE penalizes it hard (only ~20x less than the 8000-step free-run = horizon ratio); the "blind window" intuition forgot the window itself integrates. **IMPLICATION (established):** the DC minimizer b*=-g/H ~ 4e-11 (g_X=-2.93e-6 full-bank, H_XX=7.08e4), which over the free-run is ~1e-8 m of drift, 3-4 orders BELOW the observed ~1e-5..1e-4 drift -> the constant output-DC is STRONGLY CONSTRAINED and is NOT the main drift carrier; a DC/output pin would pin what the loss already pins. **REFRAMES:** the one-step lr-sweep "drift proportional to lr" is the OVERSHOOT transient of the tiny stiff b* (one Adam step ~lr >> b*~4e-11 at all tested lr), NOT a flat sloppy direction; this refines handoff item #2 ("displacement... amplified by the integrator") and REFUTES the `diagnostics-literature.md` D2/D5 "1e-12 windowed loss says nothing / objective mismatch is the disease" framing for the DC direction. **REMAINING CANDIDATE (labelled inference, NOT yet tested):** the drift is carried by a STATE-DEPENDENT ANN output (final-layer WEIGHTS = a spurious gain/feedback, output growing with a drifting state), which a constant-bias probe cannot see (null ANN output ~1e-7 >> DC minimizer ~1e-11 -> dominated by a non-constant component); aligns with the Problem-2 wrong-sign-feedback reading. **D2 COMPLETE (blindness |dDrift/dLoss| at b=1e-6, floor 6.26e-8 m): X 0.03, Theta 0.036, dX 0.30, dTheta 0.036, Y 0.78, dY 3.9.** The ratio is CHANNEL-VARYING; dY is the ONLY channel > 1 (least-constrained DC, curvature 328) -> the windowed loss is relatively BLIND to a dY DC, INDEPENDENTLY VINDICATING gantry-zero-mean's dY-DC finding. The MINIMIZER of each DC channel is tiny (g_dY=3.4e-9 -> b*~1e-11 -> ~1e-8 m drift, below observed) BUT that is not the operating point -- Adam parks the DC ~lr~1e-7 from the minimizer (curvature-blind), so the DISPLACED DC (not the minimizer) is the carrier. **CORRECTION (2026-07-24, gain_vs_dc.py, see next row): the inference in the finding doc "b* tiny => DC not the carrier => it must be a state-dependent GAIN" was WRONG (minimizer-comparison error, same mistake I made calling it a gain). The on-target intervention shows the drift is DOMINANTLY the displaced DC (~80%), NOT a gain.** CURE (agreed destination unchanged): orthogonal projection scoped to the X/Y integrator subspace (turn on `orth_beta`/`orth_observe`, keep full input + X/Y routing, verify per-axis drift), NOT a DC pin / Lipschitz / longer horizon -- because projection also removes the small (~20%) anti-damping feedback that a DC pin or SGD swap would miss. Data `curvature_sensitivity.npz`, figs `curv_D5.png`/`sens_D2.png`, full writeup `baseline-null/curvature-sensitivity-finding.md`. |
| **GAIN-vs-DC intervention (on-target carrier test), `baseline-null/gain_vs_dc.py`** (2026-07-24) | 6-state pure baseline (nx_ann=0, route 0..5), encoder frozen, TRUE-init perfect-match, noiseless baseline data, STRIDE=100 (~5.2k windows), Adam lr=1e-7 nf=400 6 ep -> TRAIN the null ANN to reproduce the drift, then CAUSAL INTERVENTION on the trained ANN: free-run T5 in 3 modes differing ONLY in the ANN's STATE input (exogenous u live in all): full (ANN reads live drifting state), frozen (ANN state input pinned to x0 = pure DC, u live), off (ANN=0). Attribution per state: DC=frozen-off, feedback=full-frozen. SECONDARY: Jacobian dw/dx_state (autograd, 9 pts along free-run) -> self-feedback J[j,j] + sign. Wiring verified: ann nz=9 = state(6)+u(3), freeze z[:, :6]. | nf 400 / free-run 8000 | per-axis DC vs feedback drift contribution + Jacobian J[j,j] | seed 0 | **Hypothesis (settle my over-claim): is the perfect-match null drift a DISPLACED DC (Adam parks ~lr from the stiff minimizer) or a STATE-DEPENDENT GAIN (feedback carried by the weights)? Predict, per the curvature test's Adam-curvature-blind reading: frozen ~ full (DC dominates). Falsify: full >> frozen (a gain).** **Outcome (2026-07-24): DC DISPLACEMENT DOMINATES -- the "gain" over-claim is REFUTED on-target.** Freezing the ANN's state input reproduces 87% of the drift (frozen/full sim-RMS 0.867). Per-axis DC vs feedback tail-drift [SI]: Y 6.10e-6 vs 1.36e-6 (fb/DC 0.22), dY 4.15e-6 vs 1.14e-6 (0.27), X 2.17e-6 vs -0.27e-6, dX 2.02e-6 vs -0.45e-6, Theta/dTheta ~0. So the displaced DC carries ~75-88%. **NUANCE (do not over-claim "pure DC" either): a small but SYSTEMATIC feedback (~20-27% on Y/dY) IS present, and the Jacobian shows it has the ANTI-DAMPING (destabilizing) SIGN on the velocity self-channels: dW_dY/ddY=+1.43e-8, dW_dX/ddX=+5.74e-9 (positive=wrong sign; sd ~1e-11 => real, not noise).** So even at perfect match Adam builds BOTH a dominant DC displacement AND a tiny wrong-sign feedback -- the latter = the likely seed of Problem-2 (the Y destabilization), subdominant in the null but growing with signal/horizon on real data. **RECONCILE: matches the literature-search B-thread (Adam = curvature-blind sign/l-infinity descent, parks a stiff DC ~lr from the minimizer; Mandt SGD residual=lr/curvature, Bernstein Adam curvature-blind, Reddi constant-lr Adam non-convergent) and vindicates gantry-zero-mean's Adam-DC-on-dY. Corrects the curvature row's "carrier is a gain".** IMPLICATION for the cure: DC part is optimizer-addressable (SGD/curvature-aware), feedback part is structural; orthogonal projection covers BOTH (removes ANN output overlap with the X/Y integrator subspace = constant force AND feedback), a DC pin or SGD swap covers only the DC. Data `gain_vs_dc.npz` (= `gain_vs_dc_012345.npz`), figs `gain_vs_dc_{attribution,jacobian}.png`. CAVEAT: single seed; null-regime magnitudes (~1e-5); the ~20% feedback is small here so its growth to Problem-2 dominance is inferred from ARTBP feedback_instrument + test_efolding, not measured on the same trained ANN across horizons. |
