Once a real residual gives nonzero gradient, SGD moves and drifts too (83x). SYNTHESIS: learning (R2) and no-drift (R4) are a SINGLE-KNOB optimizer TRADEOFF (SGD-slow = no-learn/low-drift; Adam-fast = learn/diverge) -- neither wins both, which MANDATES the structural split (regularize ONLY the non-identifiable/unexcited direction, leave the excited/friction direction free = orthogonal projection). User flagged that a matched-lr swap conflates mechanism with step size (correct) -> follow-up SGD lr-SWEEP (R2_LRSWEEP {1e-7..1e-4}) launched to confirm NO SGD lr wins R2+R4 (no sweet spot). `r2_fit_probe.py`. |

To catch ALL velocity DC needs a rank-2 pin (both dX & dY constants) = the mean penalty = kills friction (proven step 3b). So the null 'optimizer displacement' is DIRECTIONLESS Adam wandering over the whole velocity-DC space -- a fixed low-rank direction cannot catch it, and catching all of it re-invokes the friction-suppression tension. IMPLICATION: the rank-1 'measured joint-DC direction' premise (d14) is insufficient for the PURE optimizer displacement. The null may be a degenerate red herring (no friction -> no preferred DC direction -> pure wandering); the decisive test is the FRICTION case (friction gives a real direction, may separate). | **T1 PENALTY-BITE under Adam: in-loss vs implicit PROX (gates the whole soft-regularizer family), `drift-fix-trials/t1_penalty_bite.py`** (2026-07-25) | Frozen rig `e1b0511a4c`, route (3,4,5), null (perfect-match) regime, DETERMINISTIC FULL-BATCH protocol from T0.1b (fixed 256-window bank, 30 steps, no shuffling), Adam lr=1e-7. Grid re-centred to {1e3,1e5,1e6,1e7} = total shrink {0.997, 0.74, 0.055, 1e-9}, KEEPING 1e3 deliberately as a predicted-inert point because it is what distinguishes 'the penalty SATURATES' (I5) from 'the penalty was too weak'. Implementation VERIFIED exact: step-1 ratios 1.110/1.009/0.555 at beta 1e3/1e6/1e7, and 1.110*(0.909/0.9999)=1.009, 1.110*(0.5/0.9999)=0.555 match to 3 digits -> the prox multiplies the common gradient step by exactly the predicted shrinkage. The check also revealed c carries its own decaying transient (74% fall in 4 steps at inert beta), which is why the metric is the TAIL average and never an early-step reading.** **Hypothesis: step4's beta-saturation (bit-identical results 1e3->1e12) is caused by applying the penalty INSIDE Adam's adaptive preconditioner, where a large-beta penalty gradient makes the step tend to lr*sign(g) so beta magnitude stops mattering and the displacement caps at ~lr. Applying it PROXIMALLY removes beta from the gradient path entirely, so shrinkage depends on beta EXPLICITLY and saturation must vanish. PREDICT: in_loss tail \|c\| FLAT across 1e5->1e7 (saturates); prox tail \|c\| falls MONOTONELY, roughly ~1/beta at the equilibrium between the per-step gradient injection and the shrinkage. REFUTED IF: prox is also flat (the soft-penalty family is dead under Adam -> drop T6, reallocate to T2/T4, and record that as a first-class finding), or if prox bites on c but c_perp GROWS to compensate (a rank-1 dodge, step4's failure mode: one DC traded for another, the arXiv:2006.06650 projection-bias risk).** **Outcome (2026-07-25, 24/24 units, 3 seeds): CONFIRMED DECISIVELY -- in-loss SATURATES, prox BITES. I5 must be RESTATED and the soft-regularizer family is SAVED.** in_loss tail \|c\| converges to a **beta-independent AND nearly seed-independent plateau**: 2.350/2.378/2.376e-08 at beta=1e7 across three different inits, already within 4% of it at beta=1e6 (flat over the top two decades); its c_perp plateau is equally tight (1.571/1.578/1.582e-08). A quantity landing on the same value from 3 inits and across 2 decades of the knob is pinned by something other than the knob -- exactly the `lr*sign(g)` prediction (parked magnitude set by lr and geometry, not beta). prox is MONOTONE on every seed, reaching 1.880/4.602/4.343e-09 = **5.1x/27.6x/11.6x reduction**, ~6.6x BELOW the in-loss plateau. Because the prox was verified exact to 3 digits against 1/(1+lr*beta), the mechanism is not in doubt. TOTAL DC sqrt(c^2+c_perp^2) at beta=1e7: prox ~8e-09 vs control 2.07-4.98e-08 (a genuine net REDUCTION), in_loss ~2.85e-08 (within the control's own range = it RELOCATES rather than reduces). **CONSEQUENCE: I5 is not 'every soft penalty saturates under Adam' but 'a penalty saturates when applied INSIDE Adam's preconditioner'. T3a/T3b/T3d keep their delivery mechanism provided they are applied PROXIMALLY, never in-loss; T6 is NOT dropped. This revives T3b's pre-declared hypothesis (DiLaR-Soft's published failure may share step4's mechanism) from speculation to a hypothesis with measured support for its premise.** STRUCTURAL NOTE: the exact prox of a quadratic has beta->infinity limit = a HARD projection onto v's orthogonal complement, so T1 arm (c) is the beta->infinity limit of (b2) and 'soft vs hard' is a continuous DIAL here, not a dichotomy -- which lets us traverse the DiLaR soft-vs-hard axis while watching R2 instead of picking a side. **WHAT IT IS NOT: prox is NOT an R4 fix.** Crushing \|c\| up to 27.6x leaves drift at 52-130x the ANN-off floor (control 76-227x) -- better on 3/3 seeds but 1.5-3x at best, nowhere near 1x, so **the pinned DC direction is NOT the sole carrier of the drift**; this fires the Â§5.3 row 'DC lands near b* but drift persists -> RAISE T3 above T4' (caveat: drift is the DEMOTED metric on this rig). R2/R3/R5 unmeasured, so prox is a promoted APPLICATION MODE, not a fix. **SELF-CORRECTION:** mid-run I told the user 'the rank-1 dodge is real' from seed 0 alone (c_perp 3.26->9.10e-09); with 3 seeds it does NOT hold (grows 2.8x on s0, flat on s1, FALLS 2.1x on s2) -- the dodge is NOT established as systematic under prox, and the defensible claim is the total-DC reduction above. RECONCILED with step4: this REPRODUCES its saturation for the in-loss form and EXTENDS it by showing saturation is application-mode-specific; step4's 'beta saturates 1e3->1e12 bit-identical' should be read as application-specific from now on. At 84 steps, if the DC is the carrier prox should drive drift toward the floor; if the anti-damping feedback (I3) is the carrier prox should leave drift unchanged while the Jacobian stays positive.** **Outcome (2026-07-25, 6/6 units): THREE findings, the third REFRAMES the campaign. (1) prox annihilates the DC with NO DODGE, ROBUST:** \|c\| -> 5.252/5.334/7.441e-10 (24-92x reduction) from controls spanning 1.27-4.89e-08, a strikingly consistent endpoint, and \|c_perp\| does NOT grow (prox mean 4.18e-09 vs control 4.36e-09) on 3/3 seeds -- settling what T1 left open on one seed and cleanly separating the PROXIMAL application from step4's HARD rank-1 pin, which WAS dodged; the arXiv:2006.06650 compensating-bias risk did not materialise. **(2) the DC pin FIXES Y and DEGRADES X -- a per-axis TRADE, not a win:** Y -> **0.7/0.8/0.9x floor on 3/3 seeds** (at or below ANN-off, from control 4.8/1.2/2.0x), but X goes 34.8->25.2 (better), 0.7->**12.7 (18x worse)**, 2.1->**34.3 (16x worse)** -- worse on 2/3 seeds, mean 12.5->24.1x. INFERRED mechanism: v ~ (-0.75,+0.66) pins a RELATION between the dX and dY forces, so the optimizer can only serve X through the orthogonal direction, which is worse than unconstrained. **An aggregate sim-RMS would have shown Y's gain MASKING X's loss and this would have been logged as a success -- per-axis reporting is what caught it.** **(3) THE UNCONSTRAINED CONTROL IS ALREADY NEAR FLOOR AT 84 STEPS ON 2/3 SEEDS: the null-regime drift is substantially an EARLY-TRAINING TRANSIENT.** Control X 227.0->0.7 and 75.8->2.1 between steps 30 and 84 (s1, s2), Y 17.4->1.2 and 37.9->2.0; seed 0 is the non-settling outlier (X 185.7->34.8, still 35x floor) and its c PLATEAUS ~4.9e-08 where s1/s2 DECAY (s1 3.50e-08->8.15e-09). This is expected in a perfect-match null (the optimum genuinely IS zero, so decay toward it is the correct behaviour) and does NOT transfer to the with-MSD/real case where the optimum is a nonzero residual -- **but it means every drift number this project has quoted from a SHORT null run is a TRANSIENT reading, not a converged property**, and the null's honest contribution is narrower than 'training manufactures drift': it manufactures a transient excursion whose size is strongly init-dependent (1 of 3 inits still 35x floor at 84 steps). **(4) prox does NOT touch I3:** Jacobian essentially unchanged (X +1.98->+3.08, +3.34->+3.33, +3.53->+3.76e-08; Y +2.61->+2.82, +2.75->+3.13, +2.86->+2.90e-08), still positive on 3/3 seeds and both channels -- I3 survives the best available I1 treatment and needs its OWN mechanism. **CONSEQUENCE: prox is NOT PROMOTED** (R4 Y pass, R4 X FAIL, R2 unmeasured). The suspect is the RANK-1 TARGET SUBSPACE, not the proximal application (which bites and does not dodge); the natural next form is a per-row RANK-2 pin, BUT rank-2 on the velocity rows IS the already-refuted mean penalty (suppresses real friction), so it must be tested on the COULOMB rig first, never on the null. **OPEN CONTRADICTION flagged, not smoothed over:** this contradicts the project's prior per-axis attribution (`free-run-drift-has-separate-sources`: the DC dominates X, the Y destabilisation dominates Y) -- here the DC pin fixes Y and HARMS X, the opposite assignment. Prior was routing 0..5 on with-MSD checkpoints, this is routing (3,4,5) in the null, so they may be different regimes; both cannot be right and it needs resolving. ALSO: null-regime drift must from now on be reported WITH its step count and against a converged (>=84-step) control, with non-settling inits reported separately rather than averaged. Data `drift-fix-trials/data/T1b_*.json`, results `drift-fix-trials/results/T1b-prox-84-steps.md`. |

| **D5 PRECONDITIONED SHARPNESS at the trained point (does Adam's metric explain the parked constant and I3?), `drift-diagnostics/d5_preconditioned_sharpness.py`** (2026-07-25) | Frozen rig `e1b0511a4c`, null regime, route (3,4,5), true-init, encoder frozen, deterministic full-batch Adam lr=1e-7, 84 steps, IDENTICAL protocol to D2 so the retrain must reproduce `drift-diagnostics/data/ckpt_null_seed<s>_step84.pt` bitwise (verified in-run; the retrain exists only because D2 saved the network but not the optimizer state). Reads Adam's own second moment: `v_hat = exp_avg_sq/(1-beta2^t)`, `P = diag(sqrt(v_hat) + eps)` at PyTorch defaults beta=(0.9,0.999), eps=1e-8 (no betas argument is passed anywhere in the campaign). Measures, at steps 42 and 84: `lambda_max(H)` and `lambda_max(P^{-1/2} H P^{-1/2})` by power iteration on Pearlmutter HVPs, plus the P-metric Rayleigh quotient `d'Hd/(d'Pd)` along (a) the measured DC direction on the routed bias and (b) the trained anti-damping direction `grad_theta(dW_dY/ddY)`; also the routed-bias Hessian and `b*` re-measured AT the trained point with the same h-sweep and 20% stability rule D1 used. | 400 | no candidate scored (diagnostic): raw vs preconditioned sharpness against the Cohen et al. prediction `(2+2*beta1)/((1-beta1)*eta) = 38/eta = 3.8e8` | 84 full-batch steps x 3 seeds, measured at 2 step counts | **Hypothesis (D1 Â§3.6 + D2 Â§3.4 + drift-lit-sweep Â§1):** D1 measured raw `lambda_max = 3.15e3` at the zero-output init, five orders BELOW Cohen et al.'s adaptive edge-of-stability value, and their prediction is for the PRECONDITIONED Hessian, which D1 could not form (no optimizer state at the init). If the parked constant is an adaptive edge-of-stability equilibrium, then `lambda_max(P^{-1}H)` at the trained point must sit at or near `3.8e8`, and the parked offset's lr-proportionality gets a named mechanism; if it sits orders below, that mechanism is refuted and the parked position is a plain Adam limit cycle in the Bock and Weiss sense, with no curvature term. Second question, from D2 Â§3.4: the trained anti-damping direction carries only 0.6 to 6.2% of the gradient while its curvature grows as nf^3.75, and Adam is scale-free per coordinate, so the P-metric quotient along that direction says whether the preconditioner is what suppresses it. This decides whether the remedy family is the METRIC (curvature-aware or decoupled steps) rather than the gradient ESTIMATOR, which the D2 cosine numbers already put in doubt. **Outcome: EDGE-OF-STABILITY REFUTED, 3/3 seeds, and the METRIC route is refuted too.** Determinism first: the retrain reproduced D2's checkpoint **BITWISE on all three seeds**, so the deterministic full-batch protocol is now verified end to end. Preconditioned lambda_max = 8.71e7 / 2.51e8 / 1.04e8 at step 84 = **0.23 / 0.66 / 0.27 of Cohen's 3.8e8**, below threshold on every seed and still RISING from step 42 (uniform 1.42 / 1.39 / 1.42x), while raw lambda_max is **identical to five digits** across those 42 steps (5.4316e3 / 5.2008e3 / 4.9701e3). So the whole rise comes from the preconditioner SHRINKING (d'Pd falls ~30%), not from progressive sharpening. This is Cohen et al.'s **Appendix D 'Corner case'** (extremely small lr: sharpness flatlines or rises slowly below threshold; their smallest tested eta is 1e-6, ours is 1e-7), though their mechanism needs P growing and ours shrinks. The other candidate attractor is refuted on our own numbers: Bock and Weiss's bifurcation inequality gives 0.543 < 3.8, i.e. the rig sits on the STABLE side by 7x, so their 2-cycle formula never applied and the '8.9x prefactor gap' dissolves. **Metric route refuted:** in Adam's own metric the DC and anti-damping directions are comparable (P-metric quotients 2.4-2.9e7 vs 1.7-3.7e7) despite raw curvatures differing 50-70x; what separates them is gradient alignment (DC 22-68%, anti-damping 0.6-4.0%), which the preconditioner does not fix. Neither a better estimator (D2 Â§3.4) nor the existing metric reaches that direction. **b\* at the trained point** = 1.81e-9 / 5.49e-10 / 2.00e-9 against parked c = 4.61e-8 / 9.57e-9 / 3.02e-8, i.e. **15-25x mis-seating** (51x at the init), so D1's verdict survives the move to the trained point; b\* is strongly non-monotonic in step count (4-12x larger at step 42, inside the transient), a fresh I7 instance. Full write-up: `scripts/gantry/drift-diagnostics/results/D5-preconditioned-sharpness.md`. |
| **D7 ATTRACTOR OR UNCONVERGED? the 400-step null run, `drift-diagnostics/d7_long_run.py`** (2026-07-25) | Frozen rig `e1b0511a4c`, null regime, route (3,4,5), true-init, encoder frozen, deterministic full-batch Adam lr=1e-7, IDENTICAL protocol to D2/D5 in every respect except the step count: **84 -> 400**. Nothing else changes, so the first 84 steps must reproduce `data/ckpt_null_seed<s>_step84.pt` bitwise (checked in-run, as D5 did). What remains is that the iterate has NOT ARRIVED: the measured per-step move along `c` is 0.005 to 0.013 x lr (median over 9 runs, 9-22 sign reversals per 41 tail steps), so closing the residual 15-25x gap to `b*` needs tens of consistent steps, and every protocol in this campaign stops at 84. Note also beta2=0.999 gives Adam's second moment a 1000-step time constant, so at step 84 bias correction is inflating by 12.4x and the preconditioner has not equilibrated. **If `c` walks to `b*`** (~1e-9): the parked constant is a CONVERGENCE ARTEFACT, a substantial part of what this project has called "the drift" is an unfinished run, and the campaign's baseline numbers need restating. **If `c` plateaus near 3e-8** with `b*` staying orders below: there is an attractor that no current theory names (both candidates already refuted), which is a thesis-grade finding and the correct target for the next literature sweep. **If the drift-vs-floor numbers fall with step count**: I7's "much of the drift is an early-training transient" extends well past step 84 and the 84-step protocol itself is the wrong measurement point for every drift number in the campaign. **Outcome: TBD.** |

The encoder's reconstructability map is built by `gantry_linearize_and_discretize`, which is hard-coded to `Y_op = 0.0` (raises `NotImplementedError` otherwise), while the records sit at Y = -30/-15/0/+15/+30 mm plus ysweeps over ~+-0.3 m. The encoder error is therefore set by DISTANCE FROM THE LINEARISATION POINT, not by observability, which also explains why `diag20` found no clean `na` trend: longer history cannot fix a model-frame error. **(1) The per-row split inverts the naive reading.** Pooled `e_enc/e_roll = 1.33`, but that aggregate is meaningless because it is almost entirely one row: on the POSITION rows the MODEL error dominates the encoder error by 50 to 100x (X `0.01`, Theta `0.16`, Y `0.02`), while the velocity rows are comparable or encoder-dominated (dX `0.90`, dTheta `1.34`, dY `1.18`), and **dTheta alone is ~100x every other row** (`e_enc = 1.220e-01`, `e_roll = 9.133e-02`) -- matching `diag19`'s pseudo-inverse noise amplification of `115.2` on dq2. **Consequences:** (a) an UNWEIGHTED defect norm would be ~99% a dTheta penalty, i.e. it would spend the whole budget on the one row the encoder is known-worst on and the drift does not live on; `defect_scale` must therefore be per-row `1/e_roll`, which is now measured: X `8.501e-04`, Theta `6.929e-03`, Y `6.088e-04`, dX `1.298e-03`, dTheta `9.133e-02`, dY `5.075e-03` (normalised units). (b) Once scaled, the defect on the position rows prices the ROLLOUT, not the encoder, which is what D-127 needs it to do. (c) On the two near-Y=0 records (T3, T6) `|d|` is SMALLER than either component (`enc_share` 1.76 and 1.46), i.e. `e_enc` and `e_roll` partially cancel -- the C6 cancellation appearing again, here at the segment boundary. Units: `scripts/gantry/pysynth-data/results/MS1_defect_split_encoder_nodes.json`. |

(3-arm A/B), `pysynth-data/run_ms3_defect_ab.py`** (2026-07-26) | `pysynth` data (D-126, absorber ON, model==data apart from the absorber, fs_orig=fs_new=4000 so d=1). B exists so that any C-vs-A difference cannot be attributed to the longer window. | 400 (A) / 1200 (B,C) | **val sim-RMS per epoch, scored against the EPOCH-0 value** | small (see outcome) | **ACCEPTANCE CRITERIA, two RANKED gates, stated in the user's terms (2026-07-26: "we want the ANN to improve it AND most importantly dont worsen the initializaiton"):** **G1 (PRIMARY) training must not DEGRADE the init:** `max_epochs(sim-RMS) <= ~1x` the epoch-0 value. The present failure is that training is NET DESTRUCTIVE (`8.015e-05 -> 1.916e-03`, 24x, best checkpoint never leaves epoch 0), so an arm that learns nothing but HOLDS the init already passes the thing that is broken. **G2 (SECONDARY) some epoch must BEAT it:** `min_epochs(sim-RMS) <` epoch-0. G1 outranks G2. **Hypothesis:** training is destructive because the windowed loss does not price the low-frequency component that the free run integrates; the defect term prices it (Ribeiro et al. 2020 Thm 2: with defects included the objective equals the FULL-RECORD objective regardless of segment length). So C passes G1 where A fails it, and B does NOT (longer window alone is refuted). **Diagnostic to watch:** `last_defect_rms` must FALL during training -- with a non-squared (exact-penalty) norm a fixed finite weight suffices only above an unknown `rho*`, so a defect that does not fall means the run was under-weighted and is INCONCLUSIVE, not a falsification. **FALSIFIER:** if the defect DOES fall (constraint enforced) and C still degrades the init, the objective-decoupling hypothesis is dead and the destructive term is something else. **Known limitation, pre-declared:** n_seg=3 spans 0.3 s against a 12 s deliverable; `T* = 0.141 s` (`drift-visual/f09`) so 0.3 s is past drift onset but far short of the deliverable, and only a PARTIAL effect is expected. If the mechanism is real, raising n_seg should help monotonically -- that follow-up is what separates "the term works" from "it helped once". **Outcome (2026-07-26): MS3 IS INVALID AS SIZED -- THE CONTROL DOES NOT REPRODUCE THE FAILURE, ON EITHER DATASET.** Arm A on `pysynth`: epoch-0 `3.329e-02`, then `2.943e-02 / 2.459e-02 / 1.982e-02` -- monotone DOWN, **G1 = 1.000 (pass), G2 = 0.595 (pass)**. Branch-5 control, arm A on the ORIGINAL MATLAB data (`mode=augmentation`, d=5), identical settings: epoch-0 `1.125e-01`, then `1.071e-01 / 1.011e-01 / 9.645e-02` -- also monotone DOWN, **G1 = 1.000 (pass), G2 = 0.857 (pass)**. So the "training wrecks the init" failure does not appear in EITHER arm, which means **branch 5 of `docs/ms3-decision-table.md` is REFUTED (it is not the data) and B/C were never launched, because a treatment arm is meaningless when the control passes.** **Root cause of the invalidity, diagnosed:** the run was sized to fit a foreground call and that cut the OPTIMIZER STEP COUNT by ~250x. 4 train records x (48000/400) windows / batch 64 = **~7 batch updates per epoch, x3 epochs = ~21 Adam steps at lr=1e-7**, against production's 14 records x stride 10 / batch 256 x 20 epochs = **~5250 steps**. At 21 steps the iterate is still in the initial transient where the windowed loss simply descends; the documented degradation is a later-time phenomenon. **Secondary reading, real but not the headline:** the clean `pysynth` data starts **3.4x better** (`3.33e-02` vs `1.13e-01`) and improves **40% vs 14%** over the same 3 epochs, which is a D-126 signal worth re-testing once the control is valid. **Caveat that must travel with these numbers:** both `L0` values are 100x+ above the production ANN-off sim-RMS of `8.015e-05 m` and above MS2's directly-measured ANN-off free-run RMS on `pysynth` (~`1.9e-04 m`), so the selector here is very likely NOT in metres (normalised) and/or not the same quantity as the production figure. The gates are RATIOS so the verdict is unaffected, but no absolute number from this run may be quoted. **NEXT (supersedes B/C): make the control FAIL first.** Raise the update count toward production (stride down, epochs up, validation frequency down) until arm A reproduces best-checkpoint = epoch 0; only then are B and C interpretable. **FOLLOW-UP (same day, 10x steps: stride 40, 2 epochs, ~296 updates, 1377 s): THE MODEL DOES NOT MOVE AT ALL.** `Loss_val` has **0 non-finite** entries (so no divergence) and three independent metrics are bit-identical `first = best = last`: val sim-RMS `3.3294e-02`, val nf-window `3.7393e-03`, train nf-window `1.2317e-02`. Consistent with the zero-output init, where the gradient into the ANN's deep layers is EXACTLY zero (`diag9`: `t5_grad_aug_nf1 = t5_grad_aug_nf400 = 0.0`, layer-0/layer-2 grads `0.0`) and only the final layer moves at ~`lr` per step: `296 x 1e-7 ~ 3e-5`, invisible in float32. **So the local machine reaches ~300 updates per 23-minute run against the ~5250 the failure needs, and reproducing it from scratch here is several hours.** An earlier read of these runs as a NaN/divergence was wrong and is retracted: the fix to the runner (raw `Loss_val` printed with non-finite entries counted, arm refused a grade if any) shows 0 non-finite. **DECISION REQUIRED: do not reproduce, RESUME.** `simulations/gantry_subnet/augmentation_linear_map/71167/gantry_ckpt_71167.pt` (with `gantry_drift_71167_last.pth`) already exhibits best-checkpoint = epoch 0 and the K=0 DC, and `training.py` supports `resume_ckpt`, so defect-on vs defect-off can be tested WHERE THE FAILURE LIVES for one epoch of cost. Caveat: 71167 was trained on the MATLAB `augmentation` data, so the resumed test runs there and D-126's clean-data question stays separate. Alternative: move to the cluster the production SLURM jobs already use. |
| **MS5 THE FAILURE, READ DIRECTLY OFF THE PRODUCTION CHECKPOINTS (no new training)**, `torch.load` on `gantry_drift_71167_last.pth` and `gantry_drift_last.pth` (2026-07-26) | No run. Both `.pth` files are full deepSI system saves carrying the complete per-epoch `Loss_val` / `Loss_train` / `Loss_val_nf` / `Loss_train_nf` history. Load needs `scripts/gantry` on `sys.path` (they pickle `gantry_dynamic` as a top-level module). MATLAB `augmentation` data, routing (0..7), lr=1e-7, nf=400, up_sample=1, seed 42. | 400 | val **sim-RMS (full 12 s free run over V1-V4)** vs val/train **nf-window RMS (0.1 s)**, per epoch | 20 (71167) / 5 (drift_last) | **THE DEFINITIVE STATEMENT OF THE PROBLEM, and it needed no new compute.** **71167 (20 ep, 5200 batches):** val sim-RMS `1.661e-04` (ep0) -> `2.109e-02` (ep1) = **127x WORSE IN ONE EPOCH**, never returns; `argmin = epoch 0`; G1 `126.92x` FAIL, G2 `1.000` FAIL. Over the SAME run every windowed metric IMPROVES MONOTONICALLY: train loss `1.369e-06 -> 1.103e-06` (-19%), train nf-window RMS `3.807e-05 -> 3.324e-05` (-13%), **val nf-window RMS `4.386e-05 -> 3.771e-05` (-14%)**. **The val-windowed improvement is the load-bearing number: it excludes train/val overfitting as the explanation. Same validation record, same model, same data -- windowed BETTER, 12 s free-run 127x WORSE. The gap is HORIZON and nothing else.** That is objective decoupling (D-127 / Ribeiro et al. 2020 Thm 2: with no defect term the windowed objective is NOT equivalent to the long-horizon one) measured on the production run. **`gantry_drift_last` (5 ep, 130 batches) gives the DOSE-RESPONSE:** ep0 `8.060e-05` (the source of the long-quoted `8.015e-05` reference) then `1.83x / 4.69x / 6.06x / 7.16x / 9.47x`, monotone. So **130 batches -> 9.5x, 5200 batches -> 127x**: degradation scales with OPTIMIZER STEPS, which independently confirms the MS3 diagnosis that ~300-step runs cannot show the phenomenon. **Consequences:** (a) MS3's "make the control fail" prerequisite is SATISFIED by these files -- no reproduction run is needed, only a resume from `_last`; (b) `gantry_ckpt_71167.pt` is the BEST checkpoint and therefore IS epoch 0, so it can never serve as a degraded starting point -- use `gantry_drift_71167_last.pth` (`hfn`/`encoder` are live modules, not state_dicts); (c) an earlier claim that loading `gantry_ckpt_71167.pt` reproduced the failure at `9.02e-01` (8.02x) is RETRACTED -- that run had a 4-train-record trim which changed `norm`, hence the encoder built from `norm.x_all` (D-119), so it measured a normalisation-frame mismatch, not a degraded model. |
| **MS6/MS6b APPLICABILITY FALSIFIER: does the continuity defect SEE the failure?**, `pysynth-data/check_defect_sees_failure.py` (2026-07-26) | NO TRAINING. Two models whose free-run behaviour is known exactly from MS5: INIT (untrained) and `gantry_drift_71167_last.pth` (the LAST, degraded model -- not `gantry_ckpt_71167.pt`, which is the BEST checkpoint and therefore IS epoch 0). MATLAB `augmentation` data, 14 train records, up_sample=1, seed 42, V1. Checkpoint's own `hfn`/`encoder`/`norm` all taken together so no frame is mixed. Defect measured with ENCODER nodes (the real training condition) and TRUE nodes (= pure model error), 12 windows @ stride 1000, nf_seg=400. MS6b sweeps n_seg = 4/12/30/120. | 400/seg | ratio of INIT-vs-DEGRADED defect RMS, against the known 65.0x free-run ratio | 0 | **Hypothesis (pre-registered as a hard falsifier): if a 65x-worse model does not produce a much larger defect, no weight, norm or n_seg can help and the approach dies before any training run.** **OUTCOME: FALSIFIED. The defect is BLIND to the failure.** Free-run ratio **65.04x** (`1.196e-04 -> 7.781e-03 m`); defect ratio with encoder nodes **1.01x** (`2.7026e-02 -> 2.7414e-02`); with true nodes **1.78x** (`4.892e-03 -> 8.719e-03`). MS6b: n_seg 4/12/30 = coupled horizon 0.4/1.2/3.0 s gives **1.08x / 1.02x / 1.04x** -- FLAT, no trend toward 65x, so UNDER-COVERAGE is excluded as the explanation (n_seg=120 skipped, record too short). **TWO STACKED MECHANISMS, both measured:** **(1) the encoder node is noisier than the signal.** The defect sits at `2.7e-02` for BOTH models, dominated by dTheta at `6.6e-02`, while the entire failure signal in the model-error part is a `~4e-03` change -- encoder error is **~6x larger than the signal** and is IDENTICAL in both models, so it inflates the RMS while carrying no information. Root cause is MS1's finding: the encoder is an LTI reconstructability map at `Y_op = 0.0` with error scaling as `|Y|` (`corr = +0.995`). **General consequence: SUBNET-style multiple shooting, where a learned encoder eliminates the nodes, requires the encoder to be ACCURATE RELATIVE TO the accumulated model error; here it is 6x worse.** **(2) even a perfect encoder would be too weak.** True-node defects move only 1.78x against 65x, i.e. 36x under. Spectral reason: over 400 steps the model error is dominated by the absorber's oscillatory ripple at full amplitude, while the DC contributes only `t^2/2` at `t = 0.1 s`. The defect is an **RMS over all frequency content**, so the slow DC -- the component that integrates to 65x over 12 s -- is buried in broadband residual. This also explains MS6b's flatness: each extra segment adds ripple and DC in the same proportion, so SNR does not improve with n_seg. **THE GENERAL STATEMENT: the defect is a BROADBAND quantity and the failure is NARROWBAND at DC; averaging over the spectrum destroys exactly the component that matters.** **Consequence for D-127:** the continuity term as formulated (penalise `\|\|d\|\|`) is dead for this failure, and MS3's A/B is cancelled -- it would have measured nothing. What survives is the machinery and the diagnosis: penalise the LOW-FREQUENCY / mean component of `d` ACROSS consecutive boundaries, where the DC accumulates coherently and the ripple averages out. That is a different term on the same implementation, and it converges on the project's existing data-silent projection idea -- now with a measured reason rather than an assumed one. Method-correctness is NOT in question: `verify_ms_method.py` passes 5/5 (T1 zero defect on an exact model `8.6e-07`; T3 Ribeiro Thm 2 equivalence `1.19e-06`; T2a linear in injected force exponent `1.000`; T2b position defect `~nf_seg^2` exponent `1.993`; T4 minimisable `0.68x` in 30 steps). Units: `results/MS6_defect_sees_failure.json`, `results/MS6b_nseg_sweep.json`. |
| **MS7 COHERENT (DC) DEFECT vs BROADBAND RMS -- the route that survives MS6**, `pysynth-data/check_coherent_defect.py` (2026-07-26) | NO TRAINING. Same two models as MS6 (INIT vs `gantry_drift_71167_last.pth`, free-run ratio **65.0x**), same data/config (MATLAB `augmentation`, 14 train records, up_sample=1, seed 42, V1, nf_seg=400, 8 windows @ stride 1200). Compares two aggregations of the SAME boundary defects: **RMS** `sqrt(mean(d^2))` (MS6's broadband quantity) versus **\|MEAN\|** `\|(1/n) sum_j d_j\|` (the coherent/DC part), swept over n_seg = 4/12/30, with both encoder and true nodes. | 400/seg | ratio of INIT-vs-DEGRADED defect statistic; plus a coherence proxy `\|mean\|/rms` (pure ripple -> ~1/sqrt(n), pure DC -> ~1) | 0 | **Hypothesis, DERIVED not hoped: write `d_j = DC + r_j` with `r_j` zero-mean ripple. Then RMS is dominated by `\|r\|` and roughly constant in n (MS6 measured exactly that: flat 1.02-1.08x), while MEAN -> `DC + O(\|r\|/sqrt(n))`, so the DC adds COHERENTLY and the ripple averages down as `1/sqrt(n)`.** **OUTCOME: CONFIRMED, and the coherence proxy proves the mechanism directly.** True nodes: **\|MEAN\| ratio `2.34x -> 4.34x -> 13.80x`** at n_seg 4/12/30, against RMS ratio `1.57 -> 1.69 -> 1.79x`. The coherence column is the proof: for the HEALTHY model it falls `0.576 -> 0.323 -> 0.108`, tracking `1/sqrt(n)` (`0.500 / 0.289 / 0.183`) -- its defect is INCOHERENT ripple that averages away; for the DEGRADED model it stays FLAT at `0.858 / 0.831 / 0.834` -- its defect is almost entirely a PERSISTENT DC that does not. **So the two models separate cleanly by COHERENCE, and 13.80x against a 65x failure is within a factor 5, versus 1.01x for the RMS form MS6 falsified.** **The catch, and it is now the actionable finding: with ENCODER nodes the \|MEAN\| ratio reaches only `1.06 -> 1.21 -> 1.50x`.** The encoder has its OWN coherent bias (MS1: LTI map at `Y_op = 0.0`, error `corr = +0.995` with `\|Y\|`), and a coherent bias does not average out either -- it masks the model's DC. **This REVISES the MS6 assessment that fixing the encoder buys at most 1.78x: that held for the RMS metric only. On the coherent metric the encoder fix buys `1.50x -> 13.80x`, ~9x more discriminative power, so it is the ENABLING step for this route rather than a nice-to-have.** **Two-part programme, both parts now measured rather than assumed:** (1) Y-scheduled reconstructability map instead of LTI at `Y_op = 0` (`gantry_linearize_and_discretize` currently raises `NotImplementedError` for any other operating point); (2) a coherent (mean / low-frequency) defect term at large n_seg, not the RMS form. Both run on the already-verified implementation (`verify_ms_method.py` 5/5). Note this converges independently on the project's existing data-silent / orthogonal-projection direction, which selects a subspace rather than averaging over the spectrum -- now with a measured justification. Caveat: single record (V1), single seed, and the true-node arm is an ORACLE (not deployable on Telica) used here to bound what an ideal encoder could buy. |
| **MS8 + MS9 GRADIENT-USABILITY OF THE DEFECT TERMS -- BOTH VOID, both caught by their own control**, `pysynth-data/check_defect_gradient.py`, `check_grad_vs_freerun.py` (2026-07-26) | NO TRAINING. Both ask: does descending a candidate loss move the ANN in a direction that helps the 12 s deliverable? Candidates: windowed MSE (what is optimised today), RMS defect (MS6), coherent defect (MS7). Encoder FROZEN so its bias is a constant offset and only the ANN's signal is measured. MATLAB `augmentation`, V1, up_sample=1, seed 42, n_seg=12 x nf_seg=400. | 400/seg | cosine between each candidate's gradient and a reference "correct direction" | 0 | **MS8 reference = `theta_degraded - theta_init`. VOID.** Returned cosines `+0.0001` (fit), `+0.0057` (rms), `+0.0054` (coh) -- ALL BELOW CHANCE for 600 parameters (`1/sqrt(600) = 0.041`), **including the control**. The fit term demonstrably drove training from init to degraded over 5200 updates, so it cannot be orthogonal to the direction training moved. Cause: `theta_deg - theta_init` is an **Adam-ACCUMULATED DISPLACEMENT, not a gradient**; Adam's step is sign-normalised, so net displacement over 5200 steps need not align with the raw gradient at any point, least of all the endpoint. **MS9 reference = the gradient of the FREE-RUN error itself, H=4000 steps (1 s). ALSO VOID.** Pre-registered control: MS5 shows optimising the windowed loss makes the free run 127x worse, so `cos(g_fit, g_free)` had to be <= ~0; measured **`+0.4588`** at INIT. Control failed, so the other rows (INIT: rms n/a, coh `+0.080`; DEGRADED: fit `+0.339`, rms `-0.084`, coh `-0.077`) are NOT readable. **Diagnosed cause, and it is instructive: H=4000 was chosen for compute, but a DC force error grows as `t^2/2`, so its contribution at 1 s is 144x smaller than at 12 s. At that horizon `g_free` is dominated by the same broadband content the windowed loss already fits -- i.e. THE DIAGNOSTIC SHARES THE BLIND SPOT IT WAS BUILT TO MEASURE.** Secondary flaw common to both: raw-gradient cosine is the wrong object when the optimiser is Adam. **NEITHER RESULT MAY BE CITED.** Nothing is currently known about the gradients any of the three terms supply. **Proposed replacement (not yet run): a FINITE-DIFFERENCE probe -- take one training-step-sized step along `-g_candidate` and measure the resulting change in the ACTUAL 12 s free-run error, forward-only.** That asks the question at the deliverable's own horizon, needs no backprop through 48000 steps, has no proxy, and returns a signed magnitude (how much 12 s error one step of each candidate buys or costs) rather than a cosine. |
| **MS11 ORRELL TWO-RUN DISCRIMINATOR: constant force, or slowly diverging mode?**, `pysynth-data/orrell_two_run.py` (2026-07-26) | NO TRAINING. Orrell, Smith, Barkmeijer, Palmer, *Nonlinear Processes in Geophysics* 8(6):357-371, 2001, Eq. (5): `e(tau) = M(tau) e(0) + d(tau)` splits displacement error propagated by `M` (reading 6.2) from accumulated TENDENCY error `d` (reading 5). **TEST B (decisive, needs NO true states):** run twice from `x0` and `x0 + alpha*delta` with IDENTICAL inputs -- `d(tau)` is the same in both and cancels EXACTLY, so the difference isolates the propagator. **TEST A (supporting):** free-run from the recorded true state so `e(0) = 0` (printed `0.000e+00`), fit `log\|e\|` vs `log t`. Arms: INIT (zero-output ANN, so NO ANN-induced dynamics -- the control) and `gantry_drift_71167_last.pth`. MATLAB `augmentation`, V1, 48000 steps (12 s), up_sample=1, seed 42, alphas 1e-6 and 1e-5 (Orrell's step (c) linear-regime guard). | n/a | growth of `\|\|Delta\|\|`; competing fits `log\|\|D\|\| vs t` (exponential) and `vs log t` (polynomial), decided by R^2 | 0 | **OUTCOME: READING 5 CONFIRMED, READING 6.2 REFUTED.** **Test B:** POLYNOMIAL in all four cases -- R^2_poly `0.874-0.961` against R^2_exp `0.476-0.693`. Exponent is **SUB-LINEAR**: INIT `+0.342 / +0.336`, DEGRADED `+0.422 / +0.415`. Perturbations grow SLOWER than linearly, which is a marginal system with damping, not an instability. **The exponent is alpha-invariant** (`0.342` vs `0.336`; `0.422` vs `0.415`), so the linear-regime guard PASSES -- unlike MS10, which was measured in a saturated regime. Growth: INIT `4.85x / 4.32x`, DEGRADED `6.28x / 5.92x`, i.e. the ANN adds only **1.29x** of propagator amplification over a control whose ANN output is identically zero. **No exponential growth anywhere: there is no diverging mode.** **Test A:** INIT X `9.944e-05` final, poly-exp `+0.479` (R^2 0.809). DEGRADED X **`2.192e-01`** final, poly-exp **`+1.484` (R^2 0.997)**; DEGRADED Y `1.295e-01`, poly-exp `+1.677` (R^2 0.931). An `R^2 = 0.997` power law with exponent between 1 and 2 is exactly the signature of a **CONSTANT FORCE ON A LIGHTLY DAMPED AXIS**: `t^2/2` early, transitioning toward `t*f*tau` late, with `tau_X = 1.546 s` over a 12 s window. Degraded is 2200x the init on that row. **CONSEQUENCE: the fix family is settled. DC-selective / bias-aware routes are the right direction (`narrowband-literature-sweep-2026-07-26.md` Â§3.3.2 gives the two hard implementation constraints: weight the RESIDUAL not the data, and the filter must live INSIDE the training loop). The anti-damping / stabilisation family (C3, I3) is NOT what this failure needs, and `docs/narrowband-objective-problem-2026-07-26.md` Â§6.2 -- flagged there as the most serious unexcluded alternative -- is now closed.** **Script defect, does not affect the numbers:** the auto-verdict printed "mixed / inconclusive" because it used an absolute threshold (`growth < 2x`) instead of comparing arms; read the R^2 columns and the INIT control, not that line. **Caveats:** 1 record (V1), 1 seed, and Test A's reference is MATLAB's recorded truth rather than the Python model's own state, so `e(0) = 0` holds against the record, not against a self-consistent trajectory. Units: `results/MS11_orrell_two_run.json`. |
| **MS12 THE ANN'S DC OUTPUT IS SUFFICIENT AND NECESSARY FOR THE 12 s FAILURE**, `pysynth-data/measure_ann_dc_force.py` (2026-07-26) | NO TRAINING. MS11 gave the SHAPE (accumulated tendency/force error, not a diverging mode); this gives the CAUSE and the MAGNITUDE. The ANN writes additive corrections to state rows, so a nonzero MEAN on rows 3/4/5 (`dX, dTheta, dY`) is literally a constant force. Four 12 s free runs on `gantry_drift_71167_last.pth`, V1, MATLAB `augmentation`, up_sample=1: **FULL** (ANN as trained), **MEAN-ONLY** (ANN replaced by its own per-row mean along the free-run trajectory -- a genuine constant and nothing else), **MEAN-REMOVED** (ANN minus that mean), **ANN-OFF**. Means taken along the FREE-RUN trajectory, i.e. the deployed condition. | n/a | 12 s val free-run RMS [m] per arm; per-row ANN `mean`, `rms`, `\|mean\|/rms`, and the mean converted to physical units via `std_x` | 0 | **OUTCOME: THE DC IS SUFFICIENT AND LARGELY NECESSARY.** ANN-OFF `1.311e-04` (floor); FULL `7.719e-03` (58.9x floor); **MEAN-ONLY `8.708e-03` = 112.8% of FULL**; MEAN-REMOVED `2.871e-03` = 37.2% of FULL. **Replacing the entire trained network with EIGHT CONSTANTS reproduces the whole failure and slightly overshoots it -- the state-dependent behaviour contributes essentially nothing to the 12 s error.** That MEAN-ONLY EXCEEDS FULL means the fluctuating part partially CANCELS the DC, the same signature as C6 and MS1's near-`Y=0` records. Removing the mean cuts the error 2.7x but leaves it 22x the floor, so the DC is necessary-but-not-the-whole-story. **Per-row (`mean`, `\|mean\|/rms`, equivalent force):** dX `+3.46e-07`, 0.278, **`+1.67e-02 N`**; dY `+1.21e-06`, 0.233, **`+2.33e-02 N`**; dTheta `-1.44e-04`, 0.569 (torque); X `+3.14e-07`, 0.485; Y `-5.38e-07`, 0.708; **aug0 `-8.54e-05`, purity 0.956; aug1 `+9.34e-05`, purity 0.886 -- the two free latent states have collapsed to almost PURE DC.** **So 17 mN on X and 23 mN on Y destroy the 12 s deliverable: the K=0 double-integrator amplification in one number.** **CONSEQUENCE, and the obvious fix is still dead:** a zero-mean penalty on the ANN output would target exactly this, and remains RULED OUT on R2 grounds -- the real Telica residual mean is `-157.5 N` (X) and `-83.7 N` (Y) at 315 to 344 sigma (C7), so suppressing DC forbids learning real friction. **NEW AND MORE IMPORTANT -- A VALIDATION TRAP, not a solution:** on THIS simulation the true residual is the absorber, which is oscillatory and zero-mean (`dA`: dominant coupling `\|mean\|/rms ~ 1e-4`). The correct DC here is therefore genuinely ZERO, so a zero-mean constraint would LOOK like it works on the sim while being exactly wrong on real data. **The current simulation testbed cannot validate ANY DC-handling method, because it cannot distinguish "suppress spurious DC" from "suppress all DC". That is a constraint on the validation strategy, not on one candidate, and it means the injected-friction sim (`datasilent-friction-sim/`, built to step 2, steps 3a/3b never built) is a PREREQUISITE for testing anything in this family.** Speculative and NOT yet an argument: the spurious DC is `17-23 mN` against a legitimate real-data DC of `157.5 N`, ~4 orders apart, which would permit a scale-separated rule with a data-derived threshold (tied to the windowed loss's own resolution, not hand-picked) -- but the two numbers come from DIFFERENT SYSTEMS (sim vs real Telica), so the ratio is not a valid comparison yet. Units: `results/MS12_ann_dc_force.json`. |
| **A1-A4 AUTONOMOUS DIAGNOSTIC BATCH (drift-vs-diffusion, flat-vs-stiff, adjoint weight, normalisation)**, `pysynth-data/a1_drift_or_diffusion.py` + `a3a4_adjoint_and_norm.py` (2026-07-26 evening) | NO TRAINING. Four checks aimed at the two links the literature sweep showed were non-probative, plus the two cheapest never-checked items. A1: per-row ANN output mean extracted from **TEN checkpoints with real weights** (`71167_last`, `curriculum_70903` rungs 0-3 which are warm-started sequentially, `trial_ckpts_71013` trials 0-3, `diagnostics/gantry_drift_last`), binomial sign test per row. A3: adjoint weight vs frequency from the linearised model, `\|G(jw)\|^2` for a dY-row ANN correction. A4: read `ystd` / `std_x` from `compute_normalization`. | n/a | sign-test p per row; adjoint weight ratio DC/150 Hz; ystd ratio across channels | 0 | **A1 (drift or diffusion): PARTIAL, and only on Y.** 2 of 8 rows reject the random-walk null -- **Y (9 positive / 1 negative, p = 0.0215) and dY (1/9, p = 0.0215, OPPOSITE sign)**. X, Theta, dX, dTheta, aug0, aug1 are all consistent with a random walk (p = 0.75 to 0.11). **MULTIPLE-COMPARISON CAVEAT, which must travel with this: 8 tests at alpha = 0.05 expect 0.4 false positives, and NEITHER surviving row passes Bonferroni (0.05/8 = 0.00625) or Benjamini-Hochberg. So this is SUGGESTIVE, not significant.** What raises it above noise is structure rather than p: the two significant rows are the SAME AXIS with OPPOSITE signs, which a random process does not favour. Also: these are different RUNS at different `nf`, not seeds, so it is weaker than a seed-wise test. **A2 (flat vs stiff): RECONCILED, and it refutes link 3 of `flat-direction-problem-2026-07-26.md` as stated.** `curvature_sensitivity` (stiff, `d2L/db2 = 7.084e4`) probes a constant bias at `b = 0` on a 6-state baseline with `nx_ann=0`, TRUE-state init, NO ENCODER, on null (no-MSD) data. `d12` (neutral, `Delta/SE = +0.71`) probes the TRAINED checkpoint with the encoder live, on MSD training windows, as a paired full-vs-debiased FIRST-ORDER difference. Different evaluation point, different rig, different quantity -- **both stand. The loss is STIFF on a pure DC in isolation; what is FLAT is the COMBINED direction (DC plus the component that compensates it).** That is the same cancellation MS12 measured when MEAN-ONLY (112.8%) EXCEEDED FULL, and it explains why MEAN-REMOVED recovered only 2.7x instead of reaching the floor. **A3 (adjoint weight): MY OBJECTION TO THE SWEEP'S HEADLINE STANDS, overwhelmingly.** `\|G\|^2` from a dY-row correction: `4.65e+07` at 1/12 Hz, `1.00e+04` at 1 Hz, `6.42e-02` at 20 Hz, **`2.05e-05` at 150 Hz**. Ratio DC/150 Hz = **`2.27e+12`**. Adjoint (dual-weighted-residual) re-weighting of a long-horizon POSITION functional would suppress the spurious DC and simultaneously de-emphasise the 150 Hz absorber by twelve orders of magnitude -- i.e. it would essentially FORBID learning the physics. Not a G1-versus-G2 trade to be tuned; at this ratio it is disqualifying for the functional as stated. Any adjoint route must use a different quantity of interest. **A4 (normalisation mismatch): CONFIRMED, and it converges with A1.** `ystd` = X1 `3.233e-02`, X2 `3.233e-02`, **Y `1.901e-01`** m; ratio **5.88**. The training loss divides each channel by `ystd`; the selector (`sim-RMS`) does not. **So Y output error is down-weighted 5.9x in amplitude and 34x in squared error during training relative to the metric that judges the model.** **AND A1's only systematic drift is on Y and dY.** The axis the loss under-weights 34x is the axis that acquires a systematic DC. That is a mechanistic link nobody in this project had, it is a plain implementation mismatch rather than a theory gap, and it is cheap to test (train with the loss in raw metres, or with `ystd` equalised, and re-run A1). **Correlational at n = 1 axis and not proof of causation.** Units: `results/A1_drift_or_diffusion.json`, `results/A3A4_adjoint_and_norm.json`. |
| **A6 PER-AXIS DC RESISTANCE OF THE WINDOWED LOSS -- REFUTES THE A1/A4 LINK I PROPOSED**, `pysynth-data/a6_dc_resistance_per_axis.py` (2026-07-26 evening) | NO TRAINING. Tests whether the windowed loss resists a constant offset on Y much less than on X, which would make A4's normalisation mismatch the CAUSE of A1's Y-specific systematic sign. Production path (encoder live, 8 states, routing 0..7, MSD `augmentation` data, T1), 24 windows @ stride 700, nf=400. Inject a constant bias `b` on one ANN output row at a time, sweep `b`, take the symmetric second difference. **h-SWEEP WITH AN ACCEPTANCE RULE (added after v1 failed its own control): accept a curvature only where two adjacent `h` agree within 20%, else report UNRESOLVED.** | 400 | `d2L/db2` per row; ratio X/Y against the `(ystd_Y/ystd_X)^2 = 34.589` prediction | 0 | **v1 WAS COMPROMISED AND ITS CONTROL CAUGHT IT:** at a single `h = 1e-6` the Theta control returned a **NEGATIVE** curvature (`-2.52`), impossible at a minimum, i.e. the estimator was noise-limited. v1's numbers are void. **v2 (h-swept) OUTCOME:** X `7.064e+04` (accepted h 1e-7/1e-6, agreeing to 1%), Y `3.387e+04`, dX `4.856e+03`, dY `3.253e+02`; **Theta and dTheta UNRESOLVED on every adjacent pair -- correctly rejected rather than quoted.** **Ratio X/Y = 2.086, NOT the 34.589 that pure normalisation predicts. MY PROPOSED A1/A4 CAUSAL LINK IS REFUTED.** The reason is visible in numbers A4 itself printed: `std_x` is X `3.233e-02`, Y `1.901e-01` -- **IDENTICAL to `ystd`** X `3.233e-02`, Y `1.901e-01`, because `compute_normalization` derives `x_all` from `y`. The ANN's output is in normalised STATE units, so the state normalisation and the output normalisation **cancel exactly** and there is NO differential axis weighting. A4's mismatch is real in absolute terms (the loss is dimensionless, the selector is metres) but does not weight axes unequally. The residual `2.086` is approximately the channel-count effect: the P-transform maps X into two stage outputs and Y into one. **So A1's Y-specific systematic sign still has no explanation, and the "train in the selector's units" fix I proposed would not address it.** Velocity rows `dX/dY = 14.928`, also unexplained by normalisation and not pursued. **VALUABLE BY-PRODUCT -- CROSS-RIG VERIFICATION:** these production-path curvatures reproduce `baseline-null/curvature_sensitivity.py`'s null-rig values almost exactly -- X `7.064e4` vs `7.084e4` (0.3%), Y `3.387e4` vs `3.542e4` (4%) -- on a different rig, different script, different data, with the encoder live rather than absent. That independently confirms A2's reading that the windowed loss is genuinely STIFF on a pure DC, and it makes the `curvature_sensitivity` refutation of the flat-direction hypothesis robust rather than rig-specific. Units: `results/A6_dc_resistance.json`. |
| **A7 + A8: FOUR EXPLANATIONS OF THE DC REFUTED, LEAVING A WELL-POSED PARADOX**, `pysynth-data/a7_pairing_test.py` + `/tmp/a8.py` -> `results/A8_exposure_bias.json` (2026-07-26 evening) | NO TRAINING. Both on `gantry_drift_71167_last.pth`, MATLAB `augmentation`, up_sample=1, nf=400, 40 windows @ stride 700. **A7** measures the WINDOWED TRAINING LOSS (the objective actually minimised, never previously measured for these arms) for FULL / MEAN-ONLY / MEAN-REMOVED / ANN-OFF. **A8** compares the ANN's per-row output mean along the FREE-RUN trajectory against the same mean on ENCODER-ANCHORED windows, i.e. deployed versus trained distribution. | 400 | windowed loss per arm; `\|mean_free\|/\|mean_train\|` per row | 0 | **A7 PRE-DECLARED PREDICTION (`L_win(FULL)` cheapest, i.e. the DC is cheap because PAIRED with a compensator): REFUTED, and the numbers invert the picture.** FULL `7.1019e-07`; MEAN-ONLY `7.9367e-07` (**1.1175x, WORSE**); **MEAN-REMOVED `6.8889e-07` (0.9700x, BETTER)**; ANN-OFF `7.4722e-07` (1.0521x). **Removing the DC makes the TRAINING objective 3% better, and the DC alone is worse than no ANN at all.** So the DC costs 3% on the windowed loss AND 2.7x on the 12 s free run: it is bad for BOTH objectives and is not an optimisation trade-off. **A8 PRE-DECLARED READING (exposure bias: the DC is an off-distribution artefact of the free run): REFUTED.** `\|mean_free\|/\|mean_train\|` = Y `1.00`, dX `1.00`, dY `0.98`, aug0 `1.00`, aug1 `0.99` -- **the DC is identical where the model is trained and where it is deployed.** **NET: four candidate explanations are now measured FALSE.** (1) "the loss is blind to the DC" -- refuted, A6 curvature `7.06e4` on two rigs. (2) "the loss rewards the DC" -- refuted, A7, it penalises it 3%. (3) "the DC is cheap because paired with a compensator" -- refuted, A7, the pair is NOT the cheapest arm. (4) "the DC is an exposure-bias artefact" -- refuted, A8, ratio 1.00. **THE PARADOX, now well posed:** the training objective STRICTLY PREFERS the mean-removed network (3% better), the loss's own optimum in that direction is `b* ~ 6.9e-10` (D1) while the ANN sits at `~3.5e-08` (**51x beyond**), and the parameter change required is of order `1e-6` in normalised output units against an Adam step budget of roughly `5200 x lr = 5.2e-4`. Training had ample budget to remove a DC that hurts the very objective it minimises, and did not. **THE ONE REMAINING CANDIDATE, and it is the one the GAM reparameterisation targets: the DC is NOT INDEPENDENTLY ADJUSTABLE in the current parameterisation** -- the same weights that produce the (useful) AC fit produce the (harmful) DC, so the optimiser cannot reduce one without giving up the other. Note the network's output is mostly AC, not constant: purity `\|mean\|/rms` is dX `0.28`, dY `0.16-0.23`, so the DC is a small uncontrolled RESIDUE of fitting the absorber rather than something the network is trying to emit. **NEXT TEST (cheap, no training): is there a weight perturbation that reduces the output mean WITHOUT degrading the windowed AC fit?** If reducing the mean necessarily costs AC fit, entanglement is confirmed and the GAM split `g(z) = g0(z) + c` with `g0` sum-to-zero is the indicated fix; if not, the failure is simply non-convergence and the fix is a step-count or optimiser-schedule question. Units: `results/A7_pairing_test.json`, `results/A8_exposure_bias.json`. |
| **K-SWEEP + A5: THE MINIMAL REPRODUCTION, AND WHAT THE FAILURE DOES NOT NEED**, `pysynth-data/k_sweep_minimal.py` + `results/A5_aug_states.json` (2026-07-26 late) | **K-sweep:** a deliberately minimal SELF-CONTAINED testbed -- one damped mass (`m=1`, `c=0.65`, `tau=1.54 s`), an unmodelled 150 Hz sinusoid as the residual, a 600-parameter MLP writing force to the velocity row with zero-output init, windowed training (`nf=400`, Adam `lr=1e-4`, 1500 steps) and **windows initialised from the TRUE state, so no encoder anywhere**. Sweep the spring `K` over 0 / 0.395 / 39.5 / 3948 (`wn` = 0 / 0.1 / 1 / 10 Hz). **A5:** record the aug0/aug1 STATE trajectories (not the ANN outputs) over a 12 s free run of `gantry_drift_71167_last.pth`. | 400 | ANN output mean and purity; free-run RMS vs ANN-off; windowed loss vs ANN-off | 0 (A5) / 1500 steps (sweep) | **THE FAILURE REPRODUCES WITH ALMOST NOTHING IN THE SYSTEM.** At `K=0`: windowed loss **0.943x** ANN-off (the network HELPS the objective it minimises) while the 4 s free run is **1.713x** ANN-off (WORSE than emitting zero). That is the user's complaint, qualitatively, with no encoder, no LPV, no MIMO, no coupled absorber and no augmented states. **MEASURED NOT NECESSARY:** the SUBNET encoder (true-state init throughout), LPV scheduling, the P-transform/MIMO structure, the 8-state MSD absorber, and **the augmented latent states** -- the testbed has none, and A5 shows that on the real model they DECAY from `-3.802e-01` / `+3.539e-01` to `-9.221e-05` / `+1.038e-04` over 12 s (slopes `+4.3e-06`/`-4.9e-06` per s), i.e. an encoder-init transient from the random kaiming `W^a` rows (`diag18`: `x_a rms 1.76/1.66`), NOT a second integrator. Their high output purity (`0.956`/`0.886`, MS12) is a near-constant OUTPUT into states that do not accumulate it. **MEASURED NECESSARY: the integrator.** Free-run harm ratio `1.713` (`K=0`) -> `2.130` (`wn=0.1`) -> **`1.059`** (`wn=1 Hz`) -> **`0.998`** (`wn=10 Hz`): the failure vanishes with stiffness. **But the DC does NOT vanish** -- at `wn=1 Hz` the network's output is `\|mean\|/rms = 0.998`, essentially a PURE constant, while doing almost no harm (1.059x). **So marginality is an AMPLIFIER of the offset, not its cause; the offset is acquired either way.** Consequence for the thesis: adding stiffness would remove the SYMPTOM while leaving the CAUSE untouched, so drift is not evidence of a learning pathology specific to marginal plants. **CONTROL / HONESTY:** the pre-registered rule was that `K=0` must exceed 2x or nothing is readable; it gave `1.713`. The SHAPE reproduces (windowed better, free-run worse) but the MAGNITUDE does not, by ~35x against the real pipeline's 58.9x, so the K-trend is readable only as a RELATIVE trend and no absolute number transfers between testbed and pipeline. 1 seed. **PRACTICAL UNLOCK: the minimal testbed runs in MINUTES per arm against HOURS on the real pipeline**, so candidate fixes (GAM split, Huber, defect variants, optimiser changes) can be screened there first and only survivors cost real machine time. Units: `results/K_sweep_minimal.json`, `results/A5_aug_states.json`. |

Grade SINGLE, 1 seed, 1 record. Units: `dc-accumulation/results/step0_dc_sufficiency.json`. |

G1 fails -> the failure is upstream of the augmentation and stage 2 must NOT be run until it is understood. **WATCH:** `sqrt loss` rising means lr is too high; the fix is to drop lr, not to run longer. **Expected transient, do not misread it:** the lr probe (1 epoch, 260 updates per arm, V1 only, init 0.02666) showed ALL arms degrade validation before recovering, 1e-4 monotone to 0.0537 (1.9x worse, best = epoch 0), 1e-2 stuck at 0.0627 (2.4x worse, best = epoch 0), and only 1e-3 recovered inside 260 updates to 0.02598 (2.5% below init) while also reaching the lowest train loss (0.657 vs 0.929 and 0.802). Nothing diverged at any arm. So an early excursion above epoch-0 is expected here and **G1 is judged on the whole 500-epoch series, not on the first points.** **Logging note (bug found and fixed by the probe):** `fit()` saves `_last` (`fit_system.py:484`) then reloads `_best` (`:486`) via `self.__dict__ = torch.load(file)`, which REPLACES `Loss_val`/`Loss_train` with the history as of the best point; when best is not last (exactly this pathology) the series read after `fit` is truncated and the gates would pass trivially on it. Both scripts now read the series from `_last` and reload `_best` before saving the model. **OUTCOME (SLURM 73940, blade1, 2026-07-28/29, ran to completion: 500/500 epochs, 130k updates, 24 h 3 min = 2.9 min/epoch; 101 validation points, ZERO non-finite): G1 FAIL, G2 PASS. The verdict is NOT the one the branch anticipated, and the branch's "G1 fails -> the failure is upstream of the augmentation" reading does NOT apply here; see below.** Numbers: epoch-0 init `1.0326e-01 m`; **best `7.2363e-02 m`** (0.70x init, at point 30 of 101, i.e. ~epoch 150, so the best is NOT epoch 0 unlike the augmented runs); final `9.0927e-02` (0.88x init); **max `3.8662e+14`**. 69/101 points sit ABOVE init; 13 exceed 10x init and 12 of those exceed 100x, at points {11,19,20,24,34,39,40,46,47,51,53,54,62}. All blowups stop after point 62 and the last quarter is bounded (min `7.57e-02`, median `9.76e-02`, max `2.78e-01`). **(1) THE WINDOWED/FREE-RUN SPLIT REPRODUCES WITH NO BASELINE AND NO ROUTING IN THE MODEL AT ALL.** train nf-RMS `1.2847e-01 -> 1.6981e-02` (7.6x better) and val nf-RMS `9.1505e-02 -> 5.0606e-02` (1.8x better), both monotone, while full-trajectory sim-RMS oscillates and diverges. So the split documented for the augmented model (curriculum row, 71013, MS5) is **not caused by the augmentation coupling, the K=0 routing, or the zero-init**: a plain SUBNET on the same data and loss shows it too. This is the run's main scientific yield. **(2) lr=1e-3 IS WRONG AT THIS BUDGET AND THE PROBE COULD NOT HAVE KNOWN.** train sqrt-loss falls to `0.127` by point 11 (~14k updates) and then CLIMBS BACK to `0.19-0.48` for the remaining ~116k updates, i.e. the overshoot tripwire fires long after the probe stopped looking. The lr probe ranked arms on 260 updates, a horizon **500x shorter** than the run it was sizing: a direct instance of `size-a-run-by-OPTIMIZER-STEPS-not-by-wall-clock`, and the probe's own conclusion ("only 1e-3 recovers") is therefore valid only for the first ~260 updates. **(3) ABSOLUTE LEVEL: NOT COMPETITIVE.** best `7.2e-02 m` against roughly `1.6e-04 m` for the FP baseline with NO training at all, i.e. ~450x worse (indicative only: not the identical record set). 130k updates bought 30% over an untrained network. **Consequence for stage 2: HOLD, but for the instability, not for a verdict about the ANN.** Data: `scripts/gantry/full-blackbox/results/73940/` (config.json, npz with all six series, saved system) and `server-output/stage1_73940.out`. **Follow-up (next row): same budget with a cosine lr decay 1e-3 -> 1e-5 over the 130k updates**, which keeps the early descent that won the probe and targets the late instability the probe was blind to. |
| **TRUE-INIT AUGMENTATION: can the ANN learn the absorber when the initial condition is exact?**, `true-init-augmentation/true_init_train.py`, two arms lr `1e-7` and `1e-6` (row written 2026-08-02 BEFORE launch) | Same interconnect as the production run (`Gantry_State_Block` + `Linear_Output_Block` + `Static_ANN_Block` 2x16 tanh, `nx_ann=2`, routing `(0..7)`, `up_sample=1`, 4 kHz, float32, seed 42), assembled directly from `model.py:96-138` rather than through `build_model`. **Two changes and nothing else.** (1) The SUBNET encoder is GONE: every window is seeded from the truth's six physical states with ANALYTIC velocities (20 kHz RK4 replay of the 8-state truth from the rest IC, `data_exact.py`), not the record's `gradient()` rows. (2) The baseline carries the truth's static mass distribution at `delta_a = 0` (`plant_cog.py`, gates C1a-C5 all pass), so the CoG mismatch stops polluting the X and Theta rows. Batch 256, stride 100 (6664 train windows over T1-T14). | 400 (`nf_seconds=0.100`, `n_seg=1`) | **NOT sim-RMS**: there is no encoder, so the pipeline's whole-record encoder-initialised simulation does not exist. The measure is a free run of `nf = 400` from the exact IC over a stride-400 grid on V1-V4, RMS of the output error in METRES, plus the per-window DC scatter. The ANN-off value is EXACT rather than approximate: `max|w| = 0.000e+00` at init was measured, so the untrained model IS the baseline. | 100 (2600 Adam updates at 26/epoch) | **Hypothesis, and it is NOT the one the task started with.** Task item (i) ran first and **falsified the premise**: per-window re-seeding from the exact 6-state IC leaves the Y per-window DC scatter at `1.0278e-04 m` against a `3.147e-08 m` free-run floor, which is **1.0x** the `1.0273e-04 m` you get from the record's finite-difference rows. The exact velocities bought `2.6x` on X and `2.9x` on dX and **nothing at all** on Theta, Y, dTheta, dY. Mechanism established before this launch, three ways: regression of the per-window mean on `[delta_a(s), vdelta_a(s)]` gives `R^2 = 1.0000` on Y and `0.9999` on dY with fitted slopes matching the closed form `-(ma/mh)*nf*ts/2` and `-(ma/mh)` to 3.5 % and 5 %; the TRUTH model re-seeded per window from its complete 8-state IC collapses to the integrator floor (`Y 1.735e-08`); and the SAME truth model re-seeded from the exact six states with the absorber zeroed reproduces the baseline's scatter to **1.000 on all six states to four digits**. So the residual DC is the absorber initial condition and involves no baseline, CoG or LFR error at all. **What this run therefore tests is narrower and better posed than "can it learn":** `diag_static_representability.py` measured that the ideal per-step correction is `R^2 = 0.879` (V1, narrowband) but only `0.033` (T9, aprbs) explainable by a linear function of the ANN's own inputs `z = [x_norm(8), u_norm(3)]` with rows 6-7 identically zero (G6), rising to `0.999994` on BOTH records once `[delta_a, vdelta_a]` are added, and that a nearest-neighbour test rules out ANY static `f(z)` (Delta `2.14`/`1.83` against a control at `0.077`/`0.144` on identical machinery). **Pre-declared readings, scored against this run's own ANN-off value `7.1698e-05 m`:** **P1** val nf-RMS improves, because a substantial part of the correction IS statically representable on the narrowband records that dominate the training set. **P2** the per-window DC scatter on the Y channel does NOT improve materially from its `1.079e-04 m` ANN-off value, because the DC is entirely the absorber IC and no static map of `z` can produce it. **P3** hence the handoff's section-8 verdict is PERSISTENCE (rows 6-7 have no autonomous dynamics), not capacity. **Branch:** P1 and P2 both hold -> persistence confirmed end to end, and the fix is the learnable contracting `A_aug` of the GyÃ¶rÃ¶k parameterisation (coulomb-offset section 12), not encoder work. P1 fails as well -> the limit is the optimizer/loop rather than representability, and the lr probe below is the first place to look. P2 fails -> the static analysis is wrong somewhere and must be re-derived before anything is built on it. **lr probe (10 updates per arm, run before launch, and note the STAGE 1 lesson that a 10-update probe cannot rank a 2600-update run):** `1e-7` train MSE `1.139e-07 -> 1.186e-07` with a `1.53e-07` excursion at step 4, val `+0.7 %`; `1e-5` jumps `478x` at step 1 and sits at val `+333 %`; `1e-3` jumps `7e6x` and sits at val `+27833 %`. The jump scales with lr exactly as coulomb-offset section 11.4 predicts for Adam over a zero-initialised final layer (the update is gradient-normalised, so the first step is `+-lr` per weight regardless of how small the gradient is). `1e-7` is the pipeline's own documented value for `(0..7)` routing (D-101/D-102) and is kept for the primary arm to avoid adding a confound; `1e-6` is the bracket. Warmup and plain SGD would both defuse the first-step artefact and are NOT tested here. **THIRD ARM ADDED AFTER 3 EPOCHS OF THE FIRST TWO, row amended before ITS launch: lr `1e-5`.** Reason, and it is an arithmetic one that the lr probe could not have surfaced: with the final layer tracked properly (`|W_fin|`, its Frobenius norm, and `|w|`, the ANN's output on a fixed probe batch of real training states) the `1e-7` arm sits at `|w| = 4.9e-07` after one epoch while the correction it would have to produce has an RMS of `1.13e-03` on the dY row in the same normalised units. Adam's per-weight movement is bounded by `lr` per step, so over the 2340-update budget the `1e-7` arm tops out around `|w| ~ 2e-05` and the `1e-6` arm around `2e-04`: **neither can reach the required magnitude even with a perfect gradient**, so a null result from either would measure the budget and not the model. `1e-5` reaches `~2e-03` and is the only arm whose null result would mean anything about learning. It is expected to spend its first updates far above the ANN-off value (the probe measured `+333 %` at 10 updates) and is judged on the whole series, exactly as STAGE 1 had to be. **OUTCOME (2026-08-02; all three arms killed externally at 47/47/43 of 90 epochs, i.e. 1222/1222/1118 Adam updates instead of 2340. NOT lost: `_last` was written every epoch by design and the series is recovered from the streamed logs by `harvest_runs.py`, kept in `true-init-augmentation/runlogs/`).** **P1 is a null in substance, P2 is CONFIRMED, P3 holds, and the run's largest yield is a fourth thing nobody registered.** Against each arm's own exact ANN-off value `6.8486e-05 m`: best `-0.25 % / -0.65 % / -0.82 %` at lr `1e-7 / 1e-6 / 1e-5`, final `+1.02 / +2.16 / -0.16 %`, worst `+1.59 / +8.54 / +167.08 %`, and **91 % / 91 % / 93 % of all validation points sit ABOVE the ANN-off value.** A metric a model beats on 7 % of its checkpoints by under one per cent has been jittered, not learned. **P2:** the Y per-window DC improves by at most `1.40 %` across all three runs, `1.03e-04 m` before and after, exactly as the static analysis requires. **P3, and the mechanism is now measured rather than inferred:** `diag_aug_state_activity.py` reports the recurrence gain (largest singular value of `d x_aug(k+1)/d x_aug(k)`) as `0.0` untrained and `5.16e-08 / 1.02e-05 / 1.57e-04` at the three rates, against the `|lambda| ~ 0.99` a damped 150 Hz absorber needs, and it tracks the learning rate linearly rather than the data; the rows do end up carrying something (`|x_aug| ~ 1e-04`, up from exactly `0.000e+00`) but `R^2` of the best affine map onto the truth's `[delta_a, vdelta_a]` is `0.13`. **NOTE, and it corrects Â§12.2 of the coulomb-offset log: the augmented partition is NOT "rebuilt from scratch by a static feedforward net every sample".** `model.py:131` wires the ANN's input with no selection matrix (verified, `MATCH OK`), so it reads rows 6-7 and `:132` writes them: `x_aug(k+1) = h_ann(x_phys(k), x_aug(k), u(k))` is a learnable recurrence and gate G6's `0.000000e+00` is an INITIALISATION result. Gate E2 confirms it independently (perturbing only the final layer takes segment-end `x_aug` from `0.000e+00` to `2.800e-03`). The GyÃ¶rÃ¶k `A_aug` case therefore rests on WHERE the gain starts, not on whether the object exists: `sigma_A = sigmoid(alpha)` starts it at `0.5` where our wiring starts it at exactly `0`, and Adam moves a weight by at most `lr` per step. **THE UNREGISTERED RESULT, and it is the one to carry forward: the 12 s free run from the EXACT rest IC degrades `253x` to `3575x` on the same checkpoint that improved the windowed metric by `0.82 %`** (V1 `1.2979e-06 -> 4.5155e-03 m`; V2 `253x`, V3 `3575x`, V4 `313x`), with the free-run per-window Y DC going `3.56e-07 -> 4.24e-03 m`, a factor `11900`. The 120x train/select horizon gap has been demonstrated twice before with a confound each time (71167 had the encoder in the loop; STAGE 1 removed the baseline but had no informed `x0`). **Here the initial condition is exact by construction and the split reproduces at full strength, so initialisation is eliminated as an explanation of the horizon gap.** Also worth keeping: the CoG-corrected baseline's own 12 s free-run RMS from the exact IC is `1.30e-06` to `3.18e-06 m` over V1-V4, which is the number any augmentation has to beat. **Limits:** half the budgeted updates, one seed per arm (the `+-1 %` band is not resolved against seed noise, which is why the reading rests on the 91-93 % figure and on P2), and neither a warmup nor plain SGD was tried although both would defuse the first-update Adam artefact that forces the rate down in the first place. **Full write-up, including the falsified premise this run was launched under: `scripts/gantry/true-init-augmentation/IMPLEMENTATION-LOG.md`.** |
| **VARIANT B: does closing the controller around the model during the rollout let the ANN beat the encoder init?**, `closed-loop-controller/train_variants.py B` (launched 2026-08-15. **Row written AFTER launch, which violates D-090. The hypothesis and the acceptance criterion were both fixed in conversation before launch, but they were not written here first, and that is a process failure recorded rather than hidden.**) | Production config via `train_variants.make_cfg`, `ann_route_ix=(3,4,5,7)`, `nx_ann=2`, `nf=400` (100 ms), `stride=10`, `batch=256`, `lr=1e-7`, `Y_op=0.00`, 14 train records, 4 epochs, 260 updates/epoch. Loss is `ClosedLoopLossMixin` grafted at instance level: `u_model = u_data + Cfb(y_data - y_model)`, exact because `Cfb` is linear. Only `loss()` differs from production; encoder, optimiser, normalisation and shooting structure are untouched. `Y_op` changed from the previous hardcoded `0.10` because that is the VALIDATION point and matches no training record (`gtd_build_records.m`: T1-T5 at -0.30/-0.15/0.00/+0.15/+0.30, T6-T14 all at 0.00). Fixed `Cfb` is structurally correct: `generate_gantry_lti_augmented.m:90-91` builds it per record from the plant linearised at that record's `Y_op` and `gtd_run_simulation.m:33` applies it with `lsim`, so the generating controller does NOT gain-schedule along Y. | 400 | free-run sim-RMS, evaluated OPEN loop (train closed, score open) | 4 | **Hypothesis: the ANN cannot learn at `lr=1e-7` because the residual is ~1e-5 in normalised units, and it cannot use a larger rate because routing into `dX`/`dY` feeds two `K=0` double integrators (D-101/D-102). Closing the loop stabilises exactly those axes, so B should tolerate a rate one to three decades higher than A, and `sigma_max(So)=2.07` at 150 Hz means the loss is also LARGER in the absorber band. Acceptance: augmented sim-RMS materially below the baseline `5.5343e-05 m` (encoder init, ANN off, NRMS [0.000465, 0.000465, 0.000492]), toward the FP+MSD oracle.** **Falsifier fixed before launch: a flat result at `lr=1e-7` alone does NOT confirm or refute, because that rate was chosen to prevent divergence in the very axes the loop is supposed to stabilise; the run is only interpretable as a rate sweep against A.** **OUTCOME: THE RUN IS INVALID AND SUPPORTS NO CONCLUSION ABOUT CLOSED-LOOP TRAINING. Corrected 2026-08-16 after the original row over-claimed "B FAILS".** The `ClosedLoopLossMixin` code is not at fault: `u_model = u_data + Cfb(y_data - y_model)` is exact for linear `Cfb` and was verified equivalent to driving from `r` to `1e-07 m` against a `0.2 m` gap. **The EXPERIMENT is at fault: it trains a closed-loop objective and then selects checkpoints and reports on an OPEN-LOOP free-run metric. The model was never asked to be good open loop, so its being bad open loop is not a finding.** A valid test of variant B requires a closed-loop selection metric; until that exists, B is untested. The pre-registered rate escalation was cancelled, correctly, but for a reason that also does not hold: the "divergence" it was cancelled over is an artefact of the mismatched pairing. **The ONE claim these numbers do support: B's training loss is 67x smaller than A's while B's free-run error is 10x larger, i.e. the closed-loop objective and the open-loop free-run metric are strongly non-equivalent quantities.** References, both computed this run: baseline FP with ANN off, **encoder-init** (same initial information as the model, D-094) aggregate sim-RMS **`1.2244e-04 m`**, the number to beat; baseline FP true-x0 @K0 `4.2299e-04 m`, NRMS `[0.000129, 0.000130, 0.003853]`. Augmented model at init, ANN zero-init, `1.6635e-04 m`. Per epoch: `It 260 sqrt loss 1.754e-05, Val sim-RMS 0.05479` then `It 520 sqrt loss 1.736e-05, Val sim-RMS 0.1327`. **The training loss is FLAT (1 % over an epoch) while the free-run validation metric DIVERGES monotonically, `1.66e-04 -> 5.48e-02 -> 1.33e-01 m`, ending 1084x above the encoder-init reference.** Mechanism: the training loss is evaluated INSIDE the loop, where `sigma_max(So) = 0.021` at 10 Hz suppresses model error by 50x, while validation is free-run open loop with nothing hiding anything. The objective is therefore nearly invariant to parameter changes that alter free-run behaviour by two orders of magnitude, so the ANN drifts freely in directions the loss cannot see. **This is an objective-mismatch failure, not a rate failure; raising `lr` would accelerate it, which is why the pre-registered "escalate to 1e-5 then 1e-3" step was cancelled rather than executed.** It also refutes the session's own prior reasoning that closing the loop should help by stabilising the `K=0` axes and amplifying the residual at 150 Hz: both remain true of the PLANT and are irrelevant next to the loss going blind. **Secondary finding, RESOLVED by the variant A run below and NOT a bug: the `1.6635e-04 m` initial validation against the `1.2244e-04 m` baseline is a metric-scope difference, not a structural defect. `train_model`'s "Validation sim-RMS" and `evaluate_and_save`'s section A ("validation (V1)") are not the same set. Evaluated like for like, the augmented model equals the encoder-init baseline EXACTLY: aug RMS `9.573e-07 / 9.343e-07 / 2.121e-04 m` against base RMS `9.573e-07 / 9.343e-07 / 2.121e-04 m`, `+0.0 %` on all three channels. The `1.2244e-04 m` aggregate is reproduced from those three by the stated formula (`sqrt(mean of squares) = 1.2247e-04`). The earlier suspicion that the two ANN states leak into the output independently of the ANN is therefore WRONG and should not be carried forward.** Log: `closed-loop-controller/runs/variantB_lr1e-07.log`. **Next: variant A at identical settings as the control, then variant C (`So`-weighted residual, rollout left OPEN loop), which keeps the loss and the validation metric in the same space and is the only one of the three that addresses the mechanism found here.** |
| **VARIANT A CONTROL: does the PRODUCTION loss do any better at the same settings?**, `closed-loop-controller/train_variants.py A` (launched 2026-08-15, returned 2026-08-16. Wall 5948 s against B's 11545 s, a 1.94x ratio that matches `ClosedLoopLossMixin` making two `hfn` calls per step instead of one. | 400 | free-run sim-RMS, open loop | 4 | **Hypothesis: A is the control that makes B's number readable; without it a bad B is not attributable to closing the loop.** **OUTCOME: A also fails to beat the encoder init, but by a different mechanism than B, and the verdict identifies the cause as the ALREADY-DOCUMENTED Â§13 encoder `W^a` dead zone.** Val sim-RMS per epoch `5.914e-03, 5.746e-03, 1.002e-02, 8.261e-03`, i.e. one large jump off the `1.6635e-04` starting point on epoch 1 and then a non-monotone wander 47-82x above the encoder-init reference with no trend; train sqrt loss `1.172e-03, 1.164e-03, 1.159e-03, 1.154e-03`, down 1.5 % total. **The best checkpoint is therefore the UNTRAINED model: `VERDICT: ANN inactive (aug states ~0)`, `augmentation vs baseline (same init): X1 +0.0% X2 +0.0% Y +0.0%`, `x[6] enc=0.0000e+00`, `x[7] enc=0.0000e+00`, rollout aug-state `R2_raw = R2_linmap = +0.0000` on both `delta_a` and `vdelta_a`.** This reproduces run 71167's `x_enc_ann` RMS `[0, 0]` exactly and is Â§13's dead zone verbatim: `h_base` and `f_base` are wired with `selection_matrix(PHY_IX, nxd)` so nothing but the ANN reads the augmented rows, the ANN's final layer is zero-initialised so `d(ANN out)/dx = 0`, hence `dL/dx_a = 0` and `dL/dW^a = 0`; and because `Interconnect.forward` builds `xp` from zeros with no identity `x -> xp`, the augmented rows are not integrators, so `x_a(k+1) = 0` exactly and the encoder's `W^a` estimate is overwritten at the first step of every window. **The gap the augmentation would have to close, for reference: encoder-init baseline `9.573e-07 / 9.343e-07 / 2.121e-04 m` against the FP+MSD oracle (true-x0) `5.885e-07 / 5.879e-07 / 9.023e-05 m`, i.e. a factor 2.35 on Y and 1.63 on X. It closed 0.0 % of it.** **Conclusion across A and B: the failure is NOT the loss variant and NOT the learning rate. Neither objective can move the ANN because the augmented-state path is gradient-dead at initialisation, so 4 epochs at `lr=1e-7` under either loss leave the untrained model as the best checkpoint. Fixing Â§13 (break the zero-init symmetry on the ANN's final layer, or give the augmented rows an identity term so they integrate, or route something other than the ANN into them) is a PRECONDITION for any loss-variant or rate experiment being meaningful.** Logs: `runs/variantA_lr1e-07.log`, `runs/variantB_lr1e-07.log`. |
