### 2026-08-22, Phase A of the BLA initialisation: two diagnostics, no training runs

Rows added per D-090 in spirit rather than in letter: these are **not training runs**, so they carry
no `nf`, epochs or free-run RMS. They are recorded here because **the first one invalidates numbers
that other rows in this section rely on.**

| Run | What it tests | Hypothesis, fixed before launch | Outcome |
|-----|---------------|----------------------------------|---------|
| **D1b RESIDUAL IDENTITY** (`scripts/gantry/BLA-Augmentation/probe_d1_residual_identity.py`, three records, ~14 s each) | whether `cl_residual_spectrum.residual_for` returns the residual or something else. Three arms through the same integrator, rate, `up_sample` and step order, differing only in `u_fb`: `r` as `residual_for` builds it; `rho`, the same call with a **zeroed controller** (open loop on the recorded input); and `S rho`, realised as `rho` minus the auxiliary loop's response to `rho`, so `S` is never formed and the LPV scheduling is inherited rather than frozen | **`residual_for` drives the baseline with `u_data + C_fb(y_data - y_model)`, so the baseline is inside a tracking loop and `r = S rho`, not `rho`.** Falsifier: if `|r - S rho|/|r|` is O(1) on every channel the algebra is wrong and the prototype is vindicated | **CONFIRMED.** `|r - S rho|/|r|` = `0.24 %` to `52 %`, worst on `V2`, the record with most excitation and largest Y excursion, i.e. the residual IS the LPV departure. Suppression `rms(rho)/rms(r)` = **`10.1` to `642.0`**. Decisive secondary finding: `rms(rho)` spans three orders of magnitude across records (`4.19e-06` to `7.33e-04`) while `rms(r)` is nearly constant (`3.36e-07`-`4.15e-07` on X, `3.73e-06`-`3.77e-06` on Y) - **`r` is loop-limited, not disturbance-limited.** See D-153 |
| **D8/D5 RESIDUAL FIT** (`probe_d8_residual_fit.py`, clean and `--noise`, ~155 s each) | out-of-sample VAF per order of a **shared-denominator** MIMO fit of `rho` (one scalar `A(q)` for all three channels and all records, one free `x0` per record), 7 fit + 7 held-out records inside `TRAIN_FILES`, free-run simulation VAF. `VAL_FILES` untouched to avoid leaking into Phase B | **the `n_A = 28` the prototype stabilised at is an over-fit, and out-of-sample VAF will turn over well below it.** Falsifier: if in-sample and out-of-sample track each other, the over-fitting worry is wrong and the order question is about something else | **HYPOTHESIS REJECTED, and a different problem found.** `VAF_in - VAF_out` never exceeds `0.003` and is negative at six of nine orders: **no over-fitting.** The turnover is in the **spread**: median gains `0.006` from `na=8` to `na=28` while the 10th percentile across held-out record-channels falls `0.8195 -> 0.6654`. Honest order `na = 8` (`nx = 24`), ceiling `na = 16`. **But `rho(A_r) > 1` at every order from 8 up** - the fit reproduces the rigid-body drift with a near-integrator - so the Hankel spectrum does not exist and the balanced-truncation order rule is unevaluable. **Under noise it is worse**: at 1x Telica sigma `rms(rho)` rises to `2.4e-03`-`3.3e-02` **metres** (8.5 nm of sensor noise, integrated twice through the open-loop double integrators) and out-of-sample VAF is flat at `0.94` from `na = 2` to `na = 28` - **order buys nothing.** See D-154 |

**Consequence for the rest of this section.** Any row above that cites a residual-BLA number -
`157.8946 Hz`, `zeta = 0.05257`, `n_A = 28`, the `-12.0 Hz/m` Y locus, the noise sweep, or the band
recipe's `[149.90234, 164.06250] Hz` band - is **established about `S rho`, not about the
residual.** The mode **location** survives (multiplication by `S` cannot move a pole of `rho`); the
28th-order realisation, the damping and the Y-dependence do not. D-153.

### 2026-08-25, the residual BLA arms: EPOCH-2 OUTCOME and a partial data loss

Recorded the same day, after the rows below. **Read this before quoting any number from them.**

**Posterior arm reached the full 520-update budget.** Training completed `520/520` in `8 h 11 m`
and the checkpoint was written (`runs/bla_posterior_42/best.pth`, 240.9 MB, 10:13:03).

| updates | checkpoint sim-RMS [m] |
|-:|-|
| 0 | `2.186601103e-06` |
| 260 | `1.268789843e-06` |
| **520** | **`1.231200848e-06`** |

**Doubling the budget from 260 to 520 buys `3.0 %`.** That is the exact budget at which
`w1_F5_frozen_poles` reaches `3.790189e-07` and historical arm 2 reaches `3.795974e-07`, and this
arm lands at `1.23e-06`. **So BUDGET is eliminated**, which was the one candidate the 09:00 report
explicitly left open. All four now fall at matched seed, stride, `nf`, batch, routing and `eps`:
pole provenance, optimizer epsilon, state count, and update budget. The `nx_aug = 2` configuration
plateaus at `~1.23e-06` and nothing in the initialisation or the optimizer moves it; the remaining
structural differences against the `3.8e-07` family are the input map `B_a`, the readout, and the
encoder rows, which is where `AUDIT.md` section 10 already pointed.

**DATA LOSS, stated plainly.** Both training processes were killed at session teardown, not by a
crash.
- The posterior arm had FINISHED training and was inside its post-training scoring pass, so its
  weights survive and the pooled V1-V4 number is recoverable from `best.pth`. Recovery scoring was
  relaunched at 10:35 into `runs/bla_posterior_42/epoch2_score.json`.
- **The bank arm was killed at batch `519/520` of epoch 2.** deepSI writes `_best.pth` only at the
  epoch-boundary validation, so there is NO epoch-2 checkpoint for it and **its 520-update result
  is lost.** Only its epoch-1 artefacts survive. Re-running costs about 9 h.

**Consequence for the table below.** The `520`-update figure quoted here is the deepSI checkpoint
sim-RMS on `val_ckpt_data`, which is a real measurement but is NOT the pooled V1-V4 metric the arms
are accepted on; D-165 records that those two differ by a fixed `1.8e-05` relative offset. Quote the
pooled number from `epoch2_score.json` once it exists, and until then say which metric is meant.

### 2026-08-25, the residual BLA arm (row written BEFORE launch, D-090)

| Run | What it tests | Hypothesis, fixed before launch | Outcome |
|-----|---------------|----------------------------------|---------|
| **BLA-posterior seed 42** (`augmented-states/BLA/run_bla_arm.py --mode posterior`, `nx_aug = 2`, stride 10, `nf = 400`, batch 256, 2 epochs = 520 updates, **Adam `eps = 1e-16`**) | the pole the residual BLA identifies, installed in place of the ARX pole arm ii installed, at the comparator budget. The BLA chain is: residual formed at the model rate over the FULL record with no samples discarded; `f_sim` as the exogenous instrument; MIMO LPM with an explicit transient term; the replay loop removed ANALYTICALLY via `S_b^-1 = I + P_b C` (D-153: the residual is `S rho`, not `rho`, and this is the correction that rule demanded); vector fitting of the resulting `dP = P_true - P_baseline`; order selection by weighted cost | **The BLA agrees with the ARX pole, so the number will land near arm ii's `1.2e-06` and NOT near F5's `3.79e-07`, because the measured residual does not support a spread bank: one pair carries `99.8 %` of the in-band energy.** Recorded before any RMS is read, per PLAN phase 5. The one variable that is genuinely new is **Adam `eps`**: arms i/ii/iii ran at the torch default `1e-8` and the installed poles here match arm ii's to five digits, so a large improvement over `1.27e-06` is attributable to `eps` and not to the poles. Falsifier for the BLA claim: if this lands at `e-7`, then pole PROVENANCE mattered after all and the `5`-digit agreement with ARX is not the whole story | **HYPOTHESIS FALSIFIED, and informatively.** At **260 updates** (epoch 1, the run was still going at the deadline) the pooled V1-V4 free-run RMS is **`1.2687696e-06 m`**, against arm ii's `1.270080331e-06` at **1040 updates** with `eps = 1e-8`. So `eps` buys a **4x reduction in updates to reach the same level** and NOT a better level. Combined with the five-digit BLA-versus-ARX pole agreement, three candidate explanations for the `nx_aug = 2` plateau are eliminated at once: pole provenance, optimizer epsilon, and update budget. The plateau is a property of the CONFIGURATION. Checkpoint sim-RMS `1.2687898430e-06` agrees with the pooled `1.2687696e-06` to `1.6e-05` relative, the same cross-metric offset D-165 records. Ablation on the same checkpoint: `ABLATE_XA_TO_F` pooled `1.4397362e-06`, **`F = 0.186`**, so the augmented state is LIVE but **not load-bearing** by D-157's `F > 0.5`. Arm ii's three seeds also all failed that rule, so this arm reproduces arm ii on BOTH axes, score and attribution. Artefact: `BLA/runs/bla_posterior_42/epoch1_score.json` |
| **BLA-bank seed 42** (`run_bla_arm.py --mode bank`, `nx_aug = 8`, same seed, budget, stride, `nf` and `eps = 1e-16` as the row above, launched CONCURRENTLY: the machine has 12 cores and the first arm uses about two) | the same BLA, installed at the comparator COUNT instead of at the identified order. The four pairs are the pooled fit's own in-band pairs ranked by modal energy, `134.4949 / 142.7305 / 144.5016 / 157.8526 Hz`, a span of `23.36 Hz` against arm 2's `11.00 Hz`. Their provenance is the fit, not a spreading rule, which is the one thing arm iii's `zeta*f` bank and arm 2's band recipe cannot say | **Count, not pole accuracy, is what separates arm ii from F5.** The two arms differ only in `nx_aug` (2 against 8) and in which poles fill it; both carry the same dominant pair and the same `eps`. If the bank arm reaches `e-7` and the posterior arm does not, count is load-bearing and the BLA's own order selection is the wrong rule for sizing a reservoir. If both land together, count is not the variable either, and the remaining candidates are the input map and the readout. **Caveat recorded before the result: three of the four installed pairs carry under `0.2 %` of the fitted in-band energy**, so as a MODEL this bank is one pair plus three near-null corrections; it is defensible as a reservoir basis and not as an identified model, and the write-up must say which | **COUNT IS NOT THE VARIABLE EITHER, at this budget.** At the same **260 updates** the bank arm reaches checkpoint sim-RMS **`1.1936809e-06`** against the posterior arm's `1.2687898e-06`: quadrupling `nx_aug` from 2 to 8 and spanning `23.36 Hz` instead of a single pole buys **`5.9 %`**, and both arms stay in the `1.2e-06` regime rather than reaching F5's `3.79e-07`. Taken with the row above, THREE candidate explanations for the plateau are eliminated in one night at matched `eps`, seed, stride, `nf` and batch: pole provenance, optimizer epsilon, and state count. **Caveat that must travel with this number: F5 reached `3.79e-07` at 520 updates and both arms here have completed 260**, so budget is NOT yet eliminated and epoch 2 is the test. Pooled V1-V4 confirms it: **`1.1936629e-06 m`** against the posterior arm's `1.2687696e-06`, both at 260 updates, both from the untrained `2.1865622e-06`, i.e. `-45.4 %` against `-42.0 %`. Ablation `ABLATE_XA_TO_F` pooled `1.4581715e-06`, **`F = 0.266`** against the posterior arm's `0.186`: the wider bank routes MORE of the improvement through the augmented state, but both remain below D-157's `F > 0.5` and neither is load-bearing. Artefact: `BLA/runs/bla_bank_42/epoch1_score.json` |

## 13. Encoder `W^a` Dead Zone (the mirror image of Â§4)

**Symptom.** The encoder rows that reconstruct the augmented states, `Wa_psi_y` and `Wa_psi_u`
(`pre_encoder.py:404-405`), receive **exactly zero gradient at initialisation**, and over a
full run move roughly 250x less than every other trainable weight.

**Measured.** Fresh model, single forward+backward via `compute_gradient_norms`:

```
encoder.0  Wb_psi_y   1.152789e-04
encoder.1  Wb_psi_u   2.253158e-03
encoder.2  Wa_psi_y   0.000000e+00      <- exactly zero
encoder.3  Wa_psi_u   0.000000e+00      <- exactly zero
hfn.4      ANN final  6.435020e-03
```

Over run 71167's 20 epochs (5200 Adam steps, ceiling 5.2e-4 per weight): `Wa_psi_y`
max|Î”| = 2.04e-6 and `Wa_psi_u` = 7.15e-7, against 4e-4 to 6e-4 for the ANN and for `W^b`.

**Mechanism.** The augmented state rows reach the loss through exactly one path: they are part
of `x`, which is the ANN's input. Nothing else reads them, because `h_base` and `f_base` are
both wired with `selection_matrix(PHY_IX, nxd)` (`model.py:123` and `:127`). The ANN's final
layer is zero-initialised (`torch_nets.py:113-114`), so its input-Jacobian
`d(ANN out)/dx` is exactly zero, hence `dL/dx_a = 0`, hence `dL/dW^a = 0`.

This is Â§4 run backwards. There a near-zero `C_aug` on the **output** side choked the ANN's
gradient; here a zero ANN on the **input** side chokes the encoder's augmented-row gradient.

**Related structural fact: the augmented states are not integrators.** `Interconnect.forward`
builds `xp` from zeros (`interconnect.py:77-78`) and accumulates block contributions
(`:89`); there is no identity `x -> xp`. The physical rows are unaffected because
`Gantry_State_Block` returns the full next state. The augmented rows receive only the ANN, so
at zero-init `x_a(k+1) = 0` exactly and the encoder's `W^a` estimate is overwritten at the
first step of every window. Confirmed by 71167's `x_enc_ann` RMS = `[0, 0]` exactly.

**Not permanent, but slow to release.** The ANN's final layer does have gradient at init
(6.4e-3), so once it leaves zero the path opens. In 71167 it reached only â€–Wâ€–=1.94e-3 over
20 epochs at lr=1e-7, which is why `W^a` merely crept rather than staying frozen.

**Scope note against the source paper.** Hoekstra, Gyorok, Toth, Schoukens, *Encoder
initialisation methods in the model augmentation setting* defines `W^a` in Eq (8) but gives it
no initialisation method, and its simulation example (Section 4.3, Eq 37) is a **static
parallel** augmentation with **no augmented states**. So `W^a` is never exercised in the paper's
Monte Carlo, and its "model-based init converges faster" result is established for `W^b` only.
The Kaiming choice is ours and is correctly flagged HEURISTIC at `pre_encoder.py:397-399`.

**Consequences.**
1. Any `R2` reported for the augmented states before the ANN is meaningfully nonzero is
   describing a random projection, not a learned estimate.
2. Raising only the encoder learning rate does not fix this. The gate is the ANN leaving zero,
   not the encoder's step size.
3. A small nonzero ANN final-layer init would open the path immediately, but zero-init is
   load-bearing for "the model starts exactly at the baseline" and for the same-init baseline
   comparison (D-072/D-089). That trade is a decision, not a free fix.
| **STAGE 1 RERUN with COSINE lr DECAY, `full-blackbox/blackbox_standalone.py` LR_MIN=1e-5** (2026-07-29) | IDENTICAL to run 73940 in every other respect (same data, same 1:1 normalisation, nx=8, na=nb=17, nf=400, batch 256, stride 10, seed 42, learned `default_encoder_net`, no interconnect, no baseline). ONE change: `torch.optim.lr_scheduler.CosineAnnealingLR` with `T_max = 130000` optimizer steps and `eta_min = 1e-5`, passed through `init_model`'s `scheduler_kwargs` because that is where deepSI builds the scheduler (`fit_system.py:127`), and stepped ONCE PER BATCH (`:413`), which is why `T_max` is in updates and not epochs. Verified before launch that the scheduler is actually constructed and the lr decays. | 400 | sim-RMS over V1-V4, every 1300 batches (5 epochs), ~100 points | 500 (~130k updates, ~24 h at the 73940-measured 2.9 min/epoch) | **Hypothesis:** 73940's G1 failure is LATE-TRAINING INSTABILITY at a constant lr=1e-3, not evidence that the ANN, data, loss or encoder are unsound. ## 14. Controller Verification Against the Recorded Closed-Loop Signals (2026-08-16)

**Why this exists.** Every record in `data/gantry/matlab/trajectory/augmentation/` stores `r_sim`,
`u_fb`, `f_sim`, `u_total` and `y`, but the loader reads only `u_total` and `y`
(`gantry_dynamic/data.py`, `_load_u`), so `r_sim`, `u_fb` and `f_sim` had never been loaded by any
Python in this project and `Cfb` had been trusted rather than checked. Full decision and
placement argument: D-140.

**Script.** `scripts/gantry/closed-loop-controller/verify_cfb_against_records.py`, read-only.
Log: `scripts/gantry/closed-loop-controller/runs/vcfb2.log`. All **22** records (T1-T14, V1-V4,
E1-E4), per channel, full record and with the first 0.5 s discarded. The E records are included
and labelled `test`: this is a signal-identity check on the generator, not a model evaluation.

**Controller structure, measured.** Per-channel order `[3 3 3]`, **`n_FB = 9`**, identical at
every operating point. `D` diag `[2.844e+06 2.914e+06 1.509e+06]` N/m at `Y_op = 0`, i.e. direct
feedthrough. Nine distinct controllers across the records; `kappa` moves 1.5x on X1/X2 between
`Y_op = +0.30` and `-0.30`, 0.4 % on Y.

**Result 1.** `u_total = u_fb + f_sim` holds everywhere. `max abs` is exactly one float32 ulp of
`u_total` in every record and channel; relative to `rms(u_total)` it is `3.3e-08` to `3.9e-08`.

**Result 2, and the trap.** The residual `u_fb_recomputed - u_fb` splits into three regimes.

| regime | records | Y rel (settled) | explanation |
|-|-|-|-|
| exact | T3, T6, T7, T8, T13, T14, E1 (all channels); X1/X2 of T1, T2, T4, T5, V1, V3, E2 | `3.1e-08` to `1.7e-04` | at the measured storage floor (0.6-1.0x) |
| integrated DC | Y of T1, T2, T4, T5, V1, E2 | `5.8e-02` to `4.66e-01` | 99.92-99.997 % a ramp; see below |
| moving reference | T9-T12, V2, E3, E4 (all channels) | `1.1e-03` to `1.4e-02` | float32 rounding of a MOVING `r_sim`; closed by L4 |

**The mechanism behind the ramp.** `Cnorm` has a pole at `s = 0`, so the tustin controller has a
pole at `z = 1` and integrates any DC in `e = r_sim - y`. On a standstill record the Y reference is
a CONSTANT stored as `single`, so its rounding error does not average out while the moving `y`'s
does. Measured, `mean(e_Y)` against `float32(Y_op) - Y_op`:

```
 Y_op   f32 round err   mean(e_Y) obs   ratio
-0.30    -1.1921e-08    -1.2243e-08     1.027
-0.15    -5.9605e-09    -6.0298e-09     1.012
+0.10    +1.4901e-09    +1.2781e-09     0.858
+0.15    +5.9605e-09    +5.6688e-09     0.951
+0.22    -1.1921e-09    -1.2177e-09     1.021
+0.30    +1.1921e-08    +1.1544e-08     0.968
```

`T3` at `Y_op = 0.00` is exactly representable and has no ramp. The fitted slope matches
`kappa_j*w/54*mean(e_j)` to 1.3-5.2 % (T1 `-1.6076` against `-1.6525` N/s). Remove the ramp and
the Y residual lands at **0.77-0.80x the measured storage floor**. V3 is the partial case: a
Y-sweep at `Y_op = +0.10`, so the bias is time-varying and a straight line removes only 41 %.

**How the third regime was closed.** The storage floor the script measures perturbs only `y`, and
is therefore an underestimate wherever `r_sim` also moves, because a smooth signal's float32
rounding is correlated in time and lands where `|Cfb|` is 1/f large. `test_controller_exact.py`
removes the confound by re-running MATLAB's own `lsim` on identical input bits:

```
T10_aprbs_60 (regime 3)  same input bits [3.97e-10 4.66e-10 2.17e-10]  vs STORED u_fb [1.28e-03 1.21e-03 3.03e-03]
V1_standstill (regime 2) same input bits [5.89e-11 8.50e-11 4.32e-11]  vs STORED u_fb [7.55e-08 6.89e-08 2.76e-02]
L1 coefficients 9.59e-12   L2 realisation 1.46e-16   L3 end to end 1.14e-09   L4 4.66e-10   all PASS
```

**Verdict.** `Cfb` as rebuilt in Python is the controller that generated the data. **D-139's
closed-loop numbers do not need revisiting.** Coverage: the bit-exact gate covers 2 of 22
records, one from each otherwise-unexplained regime, and L1 checks coefficients at `Y_op = 0.10`
only; the `Y_op` dependence enters solely through `kappa_j`, tested on X1/X2 at all nine
operating points at `3.1e-08`.

**Lesson, and the thing to not repeat.** The acceptance criterion (`rms(residual)/rms(u_fb)
<= 1e-3` on a raw record-level residual) was derived assuming the float32 floor acts like
broadband noise, and it fails on 14 of 22 records for a controller that is exact to `4.7e-10`.
**A storage floor argued from a quantisation step is not a storage floor.** With an integrator in
the loop, a sub-ulp DC bias in the REFERENCE becomes a 1.6 N/s ramp, 47 % of `rms(u_fb)` over a
12 s record. Any future controller check compares coefficients, or runs on identical input bits,
or compares against a floor that perturbs both `r_sim` and `y`. The `residual / storage floor`
column in `verify_cfb_against_records.py` is a diagnostic, not a gate.

## 15. Augmented-State Learning: what works, what does not, and the real-data assessment (2026-08-20)

All numbers below are from artefacts in `scripts/gantry/closed-loop-controller/transient-investigation/runs/`
(`lambda_defect_calibration.json`, `input_injection_probe.json`, `bootstrap_probe.json`,
`consistency_probe.json`). Every gradient figure is measured on live training batches, not argued.

### 15.1 What works

| Component | Evidence | Status |
|-|-|-|
| Closed-loop rollout | `closed_loop_rollout` matches Kessels Eq. (5.13d), p.157, checked verbatim against the PDF | verified |
| Physical encoder `W^b` | Hoekstra Eqs. 16-17, `# THEORY:` labelled; grad `4.09e-06`, coherence `0.825` over 8 disjoint batches | trains |
| ANN correction rows 0-5 | grad `8.55e-06`, coherence `0.954`, mean pairwise cos `0.888` | trains |
| Static augmentation, end to end | free run `2.1866011e-06` untrained to `1.3933793e-06`, i.e. **-36.3 %**, reproducible across arms | works |
| D-072 baseline equality | `2.186601103417735e-06` bit-identical with D-150 and with D-151 through the production build | holds |

The 36 % comes entirely through the ANN's correction on the **physical** rows, a static function of
`(x, u)`. That path was never broken by any failure in this section.

### 15.2 What does not work, and why

The augmented states contribute nothing. The mechanism is structural, not a tuning failure.

1. **Unobserved at init.** `y` reaches `x_a` only through ANN rows 0-5, whose final layer is exactly
   zero (D-072). The Jacobian `dy/dx_a` is therefore exactly zero, so `||grad||` from `L_settled` on
   both `W^a` and `nu_log`/`theta_log` is **exactly `0.0000e+00`**, measured with and without the
   D-151 injection.
2. **Undriven at init.** `gamma * NL = 0`, so `x_a` is an autonomous ringing seeded only by
   `W^a psi`. Nothing in the data drives it, so it carries no information about the plant.
3. **The multiple-shooting defect is degenerate on the augmented rows.** With `x_a` undriven,
   `d_a,j = enc_a(psi_j) - A_aa^nf_seg enc_a(psi_{j-1})`, which is minimised by `enc_a == 0`.
   Measured: `<grad_{W^a} L_defect, W^a> = +1.983` (positive, so descent shrinks `W^a`), and under
   defect-only Adam at `lr = 1e-4` both `L_defect` and `RMS(enc_a)` fall about 2.3 % per 15 updates
   on matched batches, roughly halving within one epoch's 416 updates. This is collapse, not a gauge
   change: gauge transformations are invertible, collapse to zero is not.
4. **Without injection the pole gradient is noise.** After a 40-step warm-up that takes the readout
   off zero, over 8 disjoint batches: coherence `||mean g|| / mean||g||` = **0.086** for
   `nu_log`/`theta_log` against an isotropic-noise reference of `1/sqrt(8) = 0.354`, sign agreement
   **50 % / 50 %**. `W^a` = 0.473, also noise-like. The paths that do train sit at 0.95 and 0.83, so
   the contrast is calibrated, not absolute.

### 15.3 Four mechanisms fixed, and why the free-run number still did not move

| Fix | Did the mechanism work? | Did the result move? |
|-|-|-|
| D-150 live `A_aa` | yes, `rho` 0 to 0.9920, 34.83x loss barrier removed | no, free run `-0.665 %` |
| Burn-in `K = 100` | yes, discrimination `1.56x` to `11.56x` MS | never trained successfully |
| Multiple-shooting defect | yes, `||grad W^a||` 0 to `1.83e-01` | no, epoch 1 validation 2.8x **worse** |
| D-151 `B_a` injection | yes, pole coherence `0.086` to **`0.827`** | not yet run as an arm |

D-151 is the informative one. It converts the pole gradient from noise into a repeatable direction
(`nu_log` sign agreement **100 %, 8 of 8**; `theta_log` 62 %), which confirms the diagnosis. But the
direction points the wrong way: `dL/d(nu_log) < 0` on every batch, so descent increases `nu_log`,
which **decreases `r`** and damps the augmented mode. A randomly driven resonator uncorrelated with
the true residual is a nuisance regressor, and the cheapest local improvement is to remove it.
Cost, same measurement: the working paths lose coherence, ANN MLP `0.954` to `0.712`, `W^b` `0.825`
to `0.620`. Caveat: one snapshot, 8 batches, two arms at different points in parameter space. The
`0.086` to `0.827` contrast is far larger than that caveat; the damping direction is not yet a
result to build on.

**The pattern is the finding.** Four structural fixes, each verified to do what it was designed to
do, and the free-run error unchanged. When the augmented mode is finally given a voice in the
objective, the objective votes to attenuate it. That is not an initialisation problem.

### 15.4 Corrections to earlier records in this project

* **The burn-in citation does not hold.** `transient-investigation/RESULTS.md` section 6 attributes
  `K = 100` to Kessels' "evaluation delay `tau = n_o`". Kessels Eq. (5.14), thesis p.160, is
  `V_N = 1/(N - n_o) sum_{k=n_o+1}^{N} ||W(y_bar_k - y_hat_k)||^2` with `tau = n_o`, where `n_o` is
  the **encoder history length** (Fig. 5.4, p.158). The sum starts there because the model cannot be
  simulated earlier. There is no transient-separation argument, and the training loss `V_T` (5.12)
  has no offset at all. Burn-in is ours and must carry `# HEURISTIC:`, not `# THEORY:`. Its
  empirical case (87.9 % transient share) is unaffected.
* **Parameter-movement counts are not evidence of learning.** "108/108 `W^a` entries moved" (Arm F)
  and "3016/3130 encoder parameters moved" (epoch 1) measure gradient non-zeroness. Under Adam any
  non-zero gradient moves every parameter by about `lr` per step; the epoch-1 `Wa_delta = 4.22e-02`
  is exactly `416 x lr_enc`, i.e. fully saturated.
* **"`rho` held at 0.9920" is not evidence of health.** It held because its gradient was exactly
  zero. A frozen parameter was reported as a stable one.
* **`x_a` is a gauge, `rho` is not.** `x_a` is defined only up to an invertible transformation, so it
  has no ground truth and does not map 1:1 onto the absorber state; `W^a` is likewise arbitrary and
  random init is correct. Eigenvalues are invariant under that transformation, which is the precise
  and only sense in which the D-150 band initialisation is meaningful.
* **Superseded acceptance criterion.** "`rho(A_aa)` above 0.5 after training" is passed by a model
  whose augmented state is identically zero, and `RMS(x_a)` is gauge-dependent. Replace both with an
  **ablation test**: zero the readout's augmented columns in the trained model and re-run the free
  run. If the error does not degrade, the augmented states are decoration.

### 15.5 Readiness for noisy, nonlinear, real data

| Element | Unknown dynamics | Noise | Nonlinear |
|-|-|-|-|
| `B_a` injection (D-151) | fine, random draw | **new risk**: a driven resonant filter is a rich regressor and the readout can grow onto noise. Untested. | fine, `NL(z)` trains on top |
| Band init / `rho` (D-150) | handled: no identifiable peak means the build refuses and requires an explicit band from loop bandwidth | gated (`cl_residual_spectrum_noisy.json`) | linear pole prior, defensible as init, not as a claim |
| Multiple-shooting defect | fine | **worse**: matches two noise-driven state estimates to each other | fine |
| Burn-in | fine | neutral | fine |
| Orthogonal projection | fine | matters **more** (more mismatch, more negation pressure) | the thesis contribution |

**The dominant real-data risk is the formulation, not the initialisation.** From the 2026-08-20 sweep
(`docs/references.md`): `boroujeni2025neuralid` contains this exact rollout as its "Strategy 2" and
argues it is **inconsistent under noise unless the noise model is exact**, proposing an ICI
(dual-of-IMC) parameterisation instead; `sugie2020dualyoula` gives the classical counterpart, where
`alpha` is a noise-free filtered copy of `r` so `Q` is estimated consistently regardless of the noise
model. `landau2011adaptive` Eq. 9.78 and `karimi1998bias` explain the plateau itself: closed-loop
output error weights plant mismatch by two sensitivity functions and is structurally near-blind
below crossover.

Informativity is not the blocker: `gevers2009infomatrix` Cor. 4.4 gives informative data whenever
`K(z)` is non-zero, and `colin2020clinformativity` Thm 3 gives two discrimination channels. But
`bazanella2010mimoCL` Eq. 33 says two models are indistinguishable iff `dW_y - dW_u K = 0`, and our
augmented state at initialisation produces `dW = 0` **exactly**, so it starts inside the
indistinguishable set by construction. That is the citable form of 15.2 item 1.

**Gap with no test yet:** whether the D-151 injection causes readout overfitting under measurement
noise. The noise gate (`CL_NOISE_SIGMA`, procedure in `ANN-learning-issue/HYPOTHESES-AND-SOLUTIONS.md`
4d-4e) must be extended with a held-out free-run check before any Telica work, not after.

### 15.6 Open decisions

1. **Formulation, for the supervisors, not for this log.** Does the orthogonal-projection
   contribution survive a dual-Youla or ICI parameterisation? If yes, that line is better posed under
   noise and the machinery is already in `references.md` Q2. If no, it is out regardless of how well
   it identifies, and the remaining levers are capacity (H5, `nx_aug = 2` is one resonance; Kessels
   needed `n_ext = 14`) and H3.
2. **Drop the multiple-shooting defect** regardless of 1: measured degenerate, gradient explodes 21x
   to 1056x per group once the injection is live (pushing the balancing weight to about `1e-10`), and
   it is the term that degrades most under noise.
3. **Commit hygiene**: the burn-in citation must be fixed before this work is pushed or written up.
